# Changelog

All notable changes to hypergumbo are documented in this file. The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

- Released **tool** is at: v7.0.0
- Released **schema** is at: v0.19.0

This changelog tracks the **tool version** (package releases). The **schema version** is tracked separately in `schema.py` as `SCHEMA_VERSION`. The schema version changes when `docs/schema.json` has significant updates: breaking changes to the behavior map output format (minor bump) or additions like new type definitions for YAML validation (patch bump).

> **Looking for the reader-friendly summary?** See **[docs/RELEASE-NOTES-6.X.md](docs/RELEASE-NOTES-6.X.md)** for the audience-organized release notes (CLI users, JSON consumers, per language/framework). This file is the **implementer log**: structured, mechanism-level entries per release.

## [Unreleased]

### Changed
- **`compact`'s default selection is now the connected core, and its `--max-symbols` budget is containment-monotone (WI-zulij).** Two changes that only make sense together, both measured on the eight 2026-07-17 bakeoff maps.
  - **The default flips back to connectivity-aware.** This reverses the 2026-06-10 D12 reading ("centrality-ranked everywhere") **for `compact` only**, on evidence rather than taste: compact's output is consumed as a *graph* — it emits induced-subgraph edges, and its one in-tree diagnostic, `hypergumbo_diag.analyze_compact`, scores it on **connected components** — but a centrality-ranked prefix of a graph is frequently not connected. Measured: centrality-ranked crops carried **41.9% isolated nodes at a 25-symbol budget** and got *worse* as the budget grew (12.6 → 23.6 → 42.1 components at K=25/100/400), where the connected core stays flat (3.5 → 4.0 → 3.2) with isolated nodes at 8.5% → 2.5% → **0.3%**. `sketch` keeps centrality-ranked — it is a reading surface, not a graph. **The two surfaces therefore disagree on their top symbols by design**, which dissolves WI-zulij's original complaint rather than resolving it. `--connectivity` stays accepted as an explicit no-op (it shipped in v7.0.0); `--no-connectivity` opts into centrality-ranked selection matching the sketch.
  - **Connectivity mode gained the containment property WI-vofud thought it could not have.** `nodes(K1) ⊆ nodes(K2)` went from **55/120 budget pairs to 120/120**, pretix included. WI-vofud's recorded justification — *"bridge expansion is not a ranked prefix, so budget-containment was never that mode's contract"* — is **false**, and it had been quoted as settled for two sessions. The greedy loop never reads the budget (`max_additional` appears only in its `while` guard), so bridge expansion was *already* prefix-nested; with any budget-independent seed set, containment measured 120/120 unchanged code. What broke it was the **en-bloc, budget-sized seed preload**: admitting every seed before the first pick handed the scorer a different union-find state at every budget, producing a different *sequence* of picks rather than a longer one. Porting WI-vofud's monotone seed *quota* moves 65 violations to 66 — the seed **order** was never the problem, the preload was.
  - **The fix is an interleave, not a cap.** Seeds are metered into one budget-independent stream — fixed seed order (entrypoints by confidence, then cross-cutting endpoints by edge count) alternating 1:2 with greedy bridge picks — so the budget becomes pure truncation and a smaller budget's node list is an ordered *prefix* of a larger one's. The old adaptive entrypoint cap (1/3 vs 1/2, switching regimes on `len(eps) > max_symbols`) is deleted: alternation guarantees bridges a slot rather than leaving them the remainder, which serves the keycloak case the cap was written for more directly. **The 1:2 ratio is tuned, not arbitrary** — at 1:1 a seed can be admitted and then have both its neighbours out-scored globally, stranding it (measured 14 singletons per 100 nodes on the keycloak-shaped fixture); 1:2 drops that to 1 and 1:3/1:4 buy nothing further. Any *fixed* ratio preserves containment, so the ratio is a pure quality knob. Determinism (WI-nivuj) is untouched: the scorer, the sorted-frontier iteration and the strict `>` tie-break are reused verbatim.
  - **Cost, stated plainly.** Fragmentation is unchanged-to-better versus the old connectivity policy (components 3.5→3.5, 4.6→4.0, 3.4→3.2), and entrypoint reachability *improves* markedly at small budgets (62.0% → **80.0%** at K=25). The one real cost is a boundary artifact: truncating a stream can land on a seed, so the **final emitted node** may have no edge in the crop — measured as exactly one singleton at index 99 of 100. It cannot be special-cased away, because "don't take a seed in the last slot" is a budget-*dependent* decision and reintroducing budget-dependence is precisely what broke containment. The keycloak test's `singletons == 0` assertion is relaxed to "at most the final emitted node", with that reasoning recorded in place and a positional assertion so a singleton anywhere *else* still fails.
  - **Note on the preceding entry:** the sketch/compact filter unification below narrowed compact's default population, which cost connectivity nothing but did raise centrality-mode fragmentation (isolated 8.2% → 17.6% at K=400) by dropping `file`-kind hub nodes. With this flip that narrowing lands on `--no-connectivity`, whose stated job is now precisely *"matching the sketch"* — so dropping the same nodes sketch drops is correct for that mode rather than a regression in it. Every default consumer is strictly better off (isolated 17.6% → 0.3% at K=400).
- **`sketch` and `compact` now rank the same population — "what counts as a key symbol" has one home instead of living inside one function's body (WI-zulij).** Both surfaces advertise "the important symbols" and both already ranked with `compute_dampened_centrality` (the 2026-07-09 default flip settled the ranking *function*), yet their top-10 lists disagreed. The cause was never the ranking: sketch filtered to key declaration kinds over test-source-free edges, and compact's default `select_by_coverage` filtered **nothing at all**. The policy that made sketch's list good was a `frozenset` declared *inside* `sketch._format_symbols` with its four clauses as an inline comprehension beneath it, reachable by nothing else in the codebase — so there was no shared definition to drift from, only one function's private opinion. `KEY_SYMBOL_KINDS`, `is_key_symbol`, `key_symbols` and `production_edges` now live in `selection/filters.py`, the module whose own header already named sketch, compact and tiered JSON as its consumers.
  - **Measured on the eight 2026-07-17 bakeoff maps, through the production entry points on both sides** (`sketch._format_symbols` capturing its own `selected_symbols_out`, and `format_compact_behavior_map`): top-10 agreement **31.2% → 55.0%**, and at the shipped default budget of 100, **42.8% → 73.0%**. Eight of eight repos improved or held at K=10 and all eight improved at K=100 — SwiftyJSON 16.7% → 100%, express 18% → 74.7%, sherpa-csharp 25% → 76.1%. The 31.2% before-figure independently reproduces the 32% the item recorded.
  - **The edge filter turned out to be the dominant clause, which is the opposite of the obvious guess.** Measured per clause against sketch's ranked top-10: the symbol filter alone reaches 7.1/10, the edge filter alone 8.4/10, both together 10/10 on all eight maps. Compact had been crediting symbols for being called by their own test suites. This is why both clauses are applied rather than only the visible one, and why a denylist-only variant (which would have subsumed WI-fadab) was rejected — it reached 9.4/10 and missed two repos outright.
  - **Scope held deliberately.** `--connectivity` is exempt and keeps the broader population: its contract is bridging disconnected components, and a `file` node is frequently the only thing joining two islands, so narrowing it would remove the bridges the mode exists to find (pinned by an over-reach guard test). Narrowing happens before seed extraction rather than at the ranking call, so it also constrains which symbols can be force-included — which is the point, since a seed that is not a key symbol is precisely the noise leading compact's output today (sherpa-csharp's top five emitted nodes were all `run*.sh` file nodes; zoxide led with a shell completion and `install.sh`). The `variable` kind sits in **both** `KEY_SYMBOL_KINDS` and `EXCLUDED_KINDS` and stays in both: a Terraform variable is a real declaration surface, a CSS custom property is zero-edge noise, and which set a consumer applies is a policy choice about its own output rather than a fact about the kind.
  - **Residual, stated rather than implied:** agreement is 55%, not 100%. With the population shared, the ranked orders match exactly; what remains is that the two use different *selection policies* over that shared ranking — compact reserves half its budget for entrypoint and cross-cutting seeds, sketch uses two-phase coverage-first selection with per-file caps and language-proportional allocation. That is a separate design difference, not a filter divergence, and it is what WI-zulij still tracks.
  - **Testing.** Three red-first property tests pin the shared population (non-key kinds excluded, test-file symbols excluded, test-sourced edges not credited) plus an over-reach guard that fails if connectivity mode is narrowed, and a direct suite for the extracted policy. Two of the three property tests were **vacuous on first draft** and are documented as such in place: one passed because the coverage stop had truncated the selection to a single symbol, and the other because its fixture used `calls` — a member of `CROSS_CUTTING_EDGE_TYPES`, so every endpoint became a seed candidate and the ranking under test was never exercised. Both now carry explicit non-vacuity assertions. The sketch extraction is behavior-preserving, pinned by its existing 526 tests passing unchanged.

- **`auto-pr`'s Scenario B gate could not see an uncomputed mergeability, so it closed green PRs (INV-rahib Surface 1).** `_pre_scenario_b_gate` exists to stop the timeout and hung-run paths from closing-and-repushing a PR that already merged or is about to. Its second sub-case read `mergeable` through the shared `json_field`, which prints the empty string for a JSON `null` **and** for a missing key — so `mergeable: null` was indistinguishable from `mergeable: false`, and the gate silently ordered the close. GitHub computes mergeability *asynchronously* and reports `null` until that background job finishes: `null` is the expected value in the seconds after a push, which is exactly when this gate fires. **The sub-case was therefore inert on the live backend for as long as it existed**, and the PR-churn cycle it was built to prevent is the one WI-buniz was a downstream consequence of.
  - **New `json_bool_state` reads a JSON boolean as a tri-state** — `true｜false｜null｜absent｜other｜unreadable` — because "the forge has not computed this yet", "the forge says no" and "we could not parse the response" are three different facts that a caller is the only layer able to act on. `json_field` stays the string reader. Unknown mergeability now **re-queries `/pulls/<n>` once** (GitHub's first read is frequently what *schedules* the computation, so the second usually returns a real boolean) and, if still unknown, **defers to a green CI** rather than destroying a PR on an unread field. `false`, `absent` and `unreadable` still fall through to the close.
  - **Measured in both directions against the pre-fix library, not just asserted after** — the same payload set through the production function on both arms: `null` **1 → 0**, `true` 0 → 0, `false` **1 → 1**. Exactly one cell moves, and `false` holding is the control that matters: the fix is not a blanket "never close".
  - **Why the existing 19 tests did not catch it (L13).** They fed the gate `{"mergeable": true}` and `{"mergeable": false}` only — never `null`, never absent — while one test's docstring claimed it covered "false (or absent)". Worse, a structural test asserted the **literal source text** `mergeable=$(echo "$API_RESPONSE" | json_field "mergeable")` appears in the gate body: the exact line the fix had to change, so the test broke precisely when the bug was fixed and pointed the fixer at keeping it. It is now loosened to the dependency it was really guarding. The harness gained a *sequence* of API responses, because a stub returning one payload forever cannot distinguish "the re-query happened" from "no re-query happened".
  - **`ci-debug pr-status` now prints `Mergeable:`,** as a tri-state. This field is what the gate branches on, and until now it was unobservable through any approved script — `pr-status` read only state/merged/title/head.sha/head.ref/base.ref — which is why the defect went unverified against the real backend for as long as it did. A GitHub-shaped fixture pins that `null` renders as `null` and never as blank or `false`.
  - **Scoped honestly:** five other call sites read a boolean through `json_field`, all of them `merged`, which both forge APIs document as a non-null boolean — so they are not currently defective. The class is that the helper could not express absent/null/false, not that six sites were broken.
- **INV-rahib's invariant was unsatisfiable as written, and is refined to a declared closed set (owner ruling 2026-08-03).** The statement demanded convergence to "merged or explicit failure" with "no third state". Measured: auto-pr has **20 distinct terminal states**, and three are deliberate non-merge outcomes — `queued_vpr` (four sites, exit 0, and the vPR queue was re-affirmed the day before under WI-hajif Ruling 2), `governance_pending` (a PR left open for a human by design) and `noop_empty_queue`. No conforming implementation could satisfy the literal wording, so it was a defect in the invariant rather than in the code. The refined property: every run records a state drawn from a **declared** vocabulary, bucketed as merged / explicit failure / deliberate-non-merge, with `unknown` — the initializer, written in exactly one place — as the violation signal. Two tests enforce it in both directions: no emitted state may be undeclared, and no declared state may be unreachable (a vocabulary is allowed to be neither incomplete nor aspirational), plus a non-vacuity floor so a regex that stopped matching cannot pass the subset check trivially.
- **A convergence ledger, because the invariant had no instrument.** `.git/AUTOPR_LAST_RESULT.json` is written `open(..., "w")` — one object, overwritten every run — which is correct for what it is (WI-miriz's "did my backgrounded run succeed?" sentinel, with the overwrite pinned by its own test). But convergence is a property of the *sequence* of runs, so the item's 2026-06-17 validation criterion — "observe auto-pr converge during a real concurrent nightly" — had nothing to evaluate and went unexamined across **579 commits**. Rather than overload the sentinel with a second meaning, auto-pr now also appends one compact JSON line per invocation to `.git/AUTOPR_HISTORY.jsonl`. Append-only, best-effort by construction: an observability artifact must never turn a successful run into a failed one, pinned by a test that points it at an unwritable path.

- **`verify-claims` scopes taint flows to PRODUCTION by default, and discloses what it set aside (WI-bifob).** A claim verdict is a **disjunction** over its flows, so one flow from test code held an entire claim at `violated` and no amount of precision work elsewhere could move it. Measured on the 9-repo cohort: **2 of 18 violated claims rested entirely on a single test-sourced flow each** — `pretix/host-secret-no-network` on a `tests/e2e/conftest.py` reading an env var into `urlopen`, and `alertmanager/untrusted-input-no-network` on a `tls_transport_test.go` dialling TLS. Neither is a statement about the shipped product. Flows whose **source** is test, fixture or migration code are now excluded from the verdict; `--include-non-production-sources` restores the old behavior, so this is a default rather than a filter.
  - **The exclusions are disclosed, not dropped.** Each verdict carries an `excluded_flows` bucket keyed by reason (`test_sourced` / `migration_sourced`), reported on the **confirmed** path as well as the violated one — that is where a silent filter misleads most, because the claim reads clean and the reader has no way to learn flows existed and were set aside by a policy they did not choose. Follows the D7 shape (a production default plus labelled disclosure buckets) already used for dead-code seeds. Envelope schema `1.1 → 1.2`, additive.
  - **Measured, against a prediction registered before the run.** Which claims flip and how many: **exactly as predicted** (those two, no wrong-direction flips). Volume: predicted in the wrong unit and worth recording as such — evidence rows fell **354 → 270 (−84)** while the underlying exclusions were **692 flow-claim pairs** (675 test-sourced, 17 migration-sourced). The gap is the evidence cap: `pretix/untrusted-input-no-database` went **748 → 140 flows (−81%)** yet still displays 100 rows, because removing flows from an over-cap claim changes *which* rows are shown rather than how many. A row-level view understates this change by an order of magnitude on capped claims.
  - **`is_migration_file` is a new predicate with a DECLARED scope**, deliberately not folded into `is_test_file`: a migration is production code that ships and runs once at deploy, not test scaffolding. What the two share is only that neither describes the running application. It matches `**/migrations/` (Django), `db/migrate/` (Rails), `alembic/versions/`, `db/migration/` (Flyway) — and `versions/` **only** under `alembic/`, `migrate`/`migration` **only** under `db/`, because claiming words that common outright would silently drop real production flows from a security verdict. Anything else is an acknowledged gap, not an implied guarantee. Measured need: the existing `is_test_file` caught 84 of the 98 non-production rows and missed exactly the 14 Django migrations.
  - **Scope-axis caveat, stated because the percentages invite the wrong reading.** This is not a cohort-wide property: 74 of the 98 non-production rows are caddy (64 from a *single* file, `caddytest/integration/reverseproxy_test.go`), with pretix 15, alertmanager 7, kserve 2. Treat the effect as "one Go project's integration suite plus a Django project's migrations", not as a law about repositories.
  - **The path slot is parsed RIGHT-ANCHORED**, since it is the one colon-tolerant slot in the id grammar (ADR-0036 D1a) — `dart:dart:io:0-0:module:module` has path `dart:io`. `ir._extract_path_slot` returns `parts[1]` and is wrong for exactly this case, while `ir._parse_dangling_id` twelve lines below it is correct and documents the hazard. Rather than add a seventh naive copy, this one is correct and pinned by a test; the six existing homes and the two defective ones are filed (WI-ribuz, WI-guror, WI-nukas, WI-dosir).

- **Taint findings now report WHICH boundary a flow entered through, without relabelling anything (WI-vazal).** `AUTO_SOURCE_LABEL_MAP` collapses three io_primitives boundaries — `net_recv`, `ipc_recv` and `db_read` — into the single label `untrusted_input`, and discarded which one it was at the moment of derivation. So "a request body reached the database" and "a row read from the database reached the database" were the same fact downstream, and on an ORM-backed application the second dominates: **all 140 flows** on pretix's largest violated claim are database-read-to-database-write. `TaintSource` and `TaintFlowFinding` now carry `source_boundary`, each verdict carries a `flow_origins` breakdown, and each evidence row carries the boundary for drill-down. Envelope schema `1.2 → 1.3`.
  - **The label is deliberately untouched, and that is the whole design.** The alternative considered was giving database reads their own label — which would have been a **silent semantic change to every claim already written against `untrusted_input`**, since those flows would simply stop matching. Measured: relabelling would have flipped **3 of 16** violated claims to `confirmed` (all pretix — `untrusted-input-no-database`, `-no-host-fs`, `-no-subprocess`) with nobody told. Reporting the split instead changes **zero** verdicts and **zero** evidence rows (270 → 270 on the cohort), which the arm's instrument asserts in both directions before measuring: a run where `db_read` flows quietly stopped matching would produce a *cleaner-looking* number while measuring the option that was declined. A test pins that a `db_read`-sourced flow still matches an `untrusted_input` claim.
  - **No new vocabulary was minted.** The distinction already existed as `io_boundary`'s controlled categories and was being thrown away, so the fix carries the existing axis through rather than promoting a new `db_at_rest`/`external` one (the ADR-0024 / R1 "the fact already has a home" reversal, now at eight instances). `declared` is the bucket for YAML-declared sources (crypto, key_material, project-local catalogs), which have no boundary.
  - **This measurement corrected two of my own earlier claims, and the feature is what caught them.** Both "dropping ORM reads changes zero verdicts" and its later "correction" (that 7 non-ORM flows remained) were produced by a hand-rolled ORM predicate whose read-set omitted `exclude`, `none`, `annotate` and `select_related` — all genuine Django reads. Production classifies 100 of 100 shown rows as `db_read` where the ad-hoc predicate found 93. **Name the instrument, then verify it is the one production runs** (L53): the whole point of carrying `source_boundary` is that consumers stop re-deriving this classification badly.

### Added
- **Rust and TypeScript def/use extractors are wired into the pipeline at last, behind a gate that closes the trap for every future language (WI-tohuk).** Both shipped in March with tests and 100% coverage and have produced **zero** DDG edges ever since, because *four* independent things must all hold before an extractor has any effect and each one alone is enough to make it silently inert: its CFG mapping must declare `atomic_statement` (without it `CfgBuilder` recurses past statements down to bare identifiers, so an extractor keyed on statement types is never handed one — and the CFG stays structurally correct and fully reachable, which is why the CFG tests cannot see it); a `LanguageDdgSpec` must be registered or `build_repo_ddg` skips the language; the registering module must be **imported on the production path**, since registration is an import side effect; and the chain must actually yield an edge. Rust and TypeScript failed the first three simultaneously.
  - **A gate, not three fixes.** `test_ddg_language_wiring.py` asserts all four properties for *every* language with a registered def/use extractor, present and future, so the next language cannot rediscover the same trap. Red-first it failed 7 of 14. The production import list stays explicit — greppable, and import cost stays visible — but it is a second home for a fact the filesystem already holds, so an exhaustiveness test fails when the two disagree.
  - **Measured, not asserted.** Rust and TypeScript go **0 → 2 DDG edges** on the identical minimal fixture, and stripping `atomic_statement` returns them to 0 — the mutation proof that the check is load-bearing rather than decorative. On real repos: zoxide **273 edges / 72 symbols**, apollo-server **1,176 / 91**.
  - **`atomic_statement` swallowed Rust's control flow, and the fix is in the builder rather than the mapping.** Declaring `expression_statement` atomic — which Rust must, to get def/use on bare calls — broke three existing CFG tests, because tree-sitter parses `if c { } else { }` as `expression_statement > if_expression` and `let x = f()?;` as `let_declaration > try_expression`. The atomic declaration stopped the descent before the branch was ever classified, so the CFG lost its true/false edges while every def/use measurement kept working — the wrapper had been declared *for* def/use. In a statement-oriented grammar (Go, Python) this cannot happen: `if_statement` is its own statement and is never wrapped. `CfgBuilder` now honours `atomic_statement` only when the subtree contains no control-flow-classified descendant, so the decision is made per *occurrence* rather than per node *type* — which is all a YAML mapping can express. Measured impact: Python **identical** (61,113 edges / 6,946 symbols on pretix, unchanged), Go caddy 28,716/1,635 → **28,650/1,678** (closures now decompose), Rust zoxide 297/86 → **273/72**. Only Python feeds taint today, so no verdict can move.
  - **The new gate did not catch that regression, and now does.** Edge counts cannot see a swallowed branch. The gate gained a per-language check that an `if`/`else` still yields a block with both `true` and `false` successors — mutation-proved by removing the builder guard, which reddens Rust alone.
  - **Effect on taint findings: zero, and that is the contract.** 9-repo cohort A/B, identical inputs, re-run after the builder guard below: 54 verdict rows → 54, **270 displayed evidence rows → 270**, 311 total flows → 311, 38 confirmed / 16 violated unchanged, and **0 cells differ** across full evidence tuples (both primitives and both symbol ids). Nothing reads Rust or TypeScript DDG edges yet, so movement would have meant a leak rather than an improvement — Python's propagator receives the *whole-repo* edge list, not a language-filtered one. The arm's instrument asserts in both directions before measuring: that the wiring is live (specs registered, edges produced) and that foreign symbol ids stay disjoint from Python seed ids via the language prefix, which is *why* the null is expected by construction.
  - **The bigger gap points the other way, and is disclosed rather than implied.** The glob is `*.ts`, so JavaScript gets nothing — express, all `.js`, yields **0**. And the catalog inverts the priority: **JavaScript carries 50 sources and 83 sinks; TypeScript carries 6 and zero.** `cfg_nodes/typescript.yaml` claims in its own header to "also cover JavaScript", but it is keyed to `typescript` and the extractor registers only for `typescript`, so a `.js` file reaches neither. So this registration is *not* a taint improvement and is not presented as one: no TypeScript DDG edge can move a verdict while the language has no sinks. Class methods are also excluded, because the analyzer decides their kind slot inline (`method`/`getter`/`setter`) and re-deriving that here would put a second copy of a production classification in a consumer.
  - **`select_ddg_targets`'s docstring was false and is corrected.** It claimed "DDG analysis is expensive, so only 2-10% of functions are analyzed" while the function has **zero production callers** and `build_repo_ddg` walks every function of every file with no sampling, budget or cache — a reader sizing DDG cost from it would be wrong by more than an order of magnitude. Wiring it is deliberately sequenced after §3a, since it *reduces* which functions carry DDG data and switching it on first would make §3a's effect unreadable.
  - **Scope stated as data.** The gate is blind by construction to a language with no extractor at all, so it is a regression guard rather than a coverage claim: `javascript` (83 sinks, nothing wired) and `java` (69 sinks, a CFG mapping but no extractor to wire) are outside its population and are named in place.
- **A source-to-verdict RECALL corpus for taint — the first test in the tree that runs the real pipeline end to end.** Every other taint test hands `verify-claims` a hand-written behavior map via `--input`, so the analyzers never run; `verify_taint_claim` is fed hand-built `TaintFlowFinding` objects, and the one test that puts real source on disk asserts `rc in (0, 1)`. The consequence, stated plainly: **nothing in the suite would have failed if the pipeline stopped reporting an entire class of real flows** — which is how ten ADR-0017 capabilities came to sit inert while each was closed `done` on 100% coverage. `test_taint_recall_corpus.py` generates Python and Go repos into `tmp_path`, runs analysis → propagation → claim verification, and asserts *which* flows come out.
  - **Vacuity is structurally impossible, not merely guarded against.** A corpus of "this must NOT be reported" assertions passes trivially when the pipeline reports nothing (L17), and that is the exact failure mode here — a broken propagator makes every precision assertion greener. So each language arm puts the must-find and must-not-find shapes in **one repo** and asserts the whole finding set at once: a pipeline emitting nothing fails the must-find half in the same test.
  - **Proven by mutation, in both propagators.** Silencing `propagate_taint_structural` turns the **Go** arm red; silencing `propagate_taint_ddg` turns the **Python** arm red. That the two mutations fail different tests is itself the finding: `cli.py` routes Python through the DDG pass and everything else through the structural pass, so a Python-only regression test could never have covered the path Go runs on.
  - **The first positive control FAILED, and that is why the corpus exists in this shape (L9).** A source and a sink under a common caller — `main` calls `handler`, `main` calls `write_it` — reports **nothing**: propagation seeds at the source's *caller* and walks forward only, so two siblings are never connected. `reverse_adj` is built in `propagate_taint_structural` and never read. Recorded as `xfail(strict=True)` rather than as an assertion of the wrong behaviour, so it XPASSes and demands attention the moment source-side propagation lands (L19) — the imperative `pytest.xfail()` form can never XPASS and would silently disable the signal.
  - **Catalog entries are chosen, not assumed.** Every source and sink is module-qualified and verified absent from its language's `ambiguous_names` before use (`asyncio.start_server` → `os.mkdir`; `net/http.ListenAndServe` → `os.WriteFile`). INV-karud's own first control returned a clean zero because it used a bare ambiguous name and a chained call the analyzer does not emit.
  - **Generated into `tmp_path`, deliberately.** A fixture corpus analyzed in place measures the pipeline's own test-path heuristics rather than the producer (L20/L21) — and since `verify-claims` now excludes test-sourced flows by default, a corpus committed under a directory named `fixtures` would be excluded from its own verdicts.
- **Go def/use extractor, and the statement-level Go CFG it needs (ADR-0017 §1c).** `go.yaml` gains `atomic_statement`; without it `CfgBuilder` recurses past `short_var_declaration` / `assignment_statement` down to bare identifiers, so an extractor keyed on statement node types is never handed one — measured, Go's CFG statements were `{identifier, field_identifier, interpreted_string_literal_content}`. `python.yaml` was the **only** mapping that declared this, which is why every non-Python extractor emitted zero DDG edges. The new extractor covers short-var and var/const declarations, assignment (including selector and index targets, and augmented forms that read their target), inc/dec, send, `go` statements and bare calls; unrecognised nodes fall back to "every identifier is a use", which can over-link but can never invent a definition that drops a real flow.
  - **The repo-level DDG builder moved out of `cli.py` and stopped being Python-only.** `_build_python_ddg_for_verify_claims` was a ~200-line repo-walking analysis pipeline inside a 10,313-line module whose own docstring scopes it to *"argument parsing and dispatching to command handlers"*. Its name encoded both faults — a hardcoded language and a single consumer — and both were load-bearing: the sole call site passed the literal `"python"`, which is one of the two independent reasons the Rust and TypeScript extractors had no production caller. Now `hypergumbo_core.ddg_build` owns walk → parse → CFG → reaching-defs → aggregate, parameterized by a registered `LanguageDdgSpec` (core owns the registry, language packages register into it — the idiom `register_def_use_extractor` already uses, needed here because naming a Go method requires a receiver helper that lives in the language package). **Verified pure move:** 22,725 edges, 1,306 symbols, 403 hint-callers, edge digest `97f8fea3700e82bb` and symbol digest `51cb00bdad717e18` identical before and after. That measurement was taken on a Python-only corpus; on a polyglot repo the aggregated edge list can now carry other languages' edges into Python propagation, which is inert today but is *not* covered by the digest.
  - **`DdgEdge` gains `symbol_id`**, stamped at its single construction site. Block ids are function-local, so an aggregated repo-level edge list cannot attribute an edge without it. This is a **prerequisite** for §3a, not a fix.
  - **Effect on taint findings: zero, and measured rather than assumed** — 149 flows across the 9-repo cohort, 0 added, 0 removed. It cannot be otherwise until §3a exists (see Fixed). On caddy the pipeline now builds **28,716 Go DDG edges over 1,635 symbols** with ids matching the Go analyzer's, and every one is discarded: the propagation gate stays `lang == "python"` deliberately, carrying a comment that says so, because widening it today would stamp `analysis_method="ddg"` on structurally-derived findings — a security tool asserting a precision it did not use. Leaving the build in place rather than gating it costs ~1.9s per caddy-sized repo and buys attributability: when §3a lands it changes exactly one variable.
  - **Non-vacuity is asserted at the pipeline level, not the extractor level** — a Go function must yield ≥1 DDG edge, and 0 when `atomic_statement` is removed. The extractor being correct in isolation is precisely what the preceding layers of this bug looked like.
  - **Disclosed gaps, tracked:** `if err := do(); err != nil` and `for i := 0; ...` initializers and `range` clauses are not reached by the CFG mapping's conditional/loop hooks, so those bindings remain invisible to def/use; pointer aliasing is not modelled.

### Removed
- **The self-hosted-Forgejo CI failover is retired, not repointed (WI-hajif).** With the GitHub migration live (`origin` is github.com; Codeberg is now a passive `git push --mirror` target), the failover's job was taken over by the validated 2026-07-23 outage SOP: `woodpecker exec` runs the entire gate offline with no server and no network (drill: 10,331 passed, 100% coverage), and a plain bare git repo we control holds commits until the forge returns. Standing a Forgejo back up as the fallback would also re-introduce the exact vendor dependency the migration exists to remove. Deleted: `scripts/ci-failover` (981 lines), `scripts/lib/failover-git-shim.sh`, `docs/sops/CI_FAILOVER.md`, and `apply_failover_overrides` from the forge library.
  - **It was a layer, not a script, and the entry point was the small part.** The `selfh`/`CI_FAILOVER_ACTIVE` awareness also lived in `auto-pr` (push remote, flush remote, ops-diff base, merged-PR recording), `merge-pr` (pull remote, token, PR URLs), `ci-debug`, `smart-test` (merge-base re-anchoring), `install-hooks`, `.githooks/pre-push` (a push guard), `.githooks/test_hooks.sh` (a 77-line test block), `.agent/hooks/_shared/stop_logic.sh`, and the tracker package (`_FailoverState` + `_detect_failover` + three consumers). Each limb reads like ordinary remote-selection logic in isolation, which is why the removal ships with a **recurrence gate** (`tests/test_ci_failover_retired.py`) rather than trusting a one-time sweep.
  - **The gate's own first draft was too narrow, and that is the point of recording it.** Scanning only `scripts/` and `.githooks/`, it went green while `stop_logic.sh` still *called* the deleted `apply_failover_overrides` and the tracker package still parsed the flag file. It now scans `.agent/hooks` and `packages/*/src` as well, matches the shell-variable spelling (`FAILOVER_ACTIVE`) separately from the flag-file spelling (`CI_FAILOVER_ACTIVE`), and carries a non-vacuity floor asserting it actually *reaches* those roots — a gate's "0 violations" is only ever scoped to what it scans.
  - **Three green checks each hid a broken edit, in three different ways.** A block transformer that did not understand `elif` collapsed two if/elif/else chains and deleted the `HG_GITHUB_TOKEN` credential branch and the GitHub PR-URL branch — **`bash -n` passed**, and every GitHub API call would have lost its token. Removing `_detect_failover` left `failover.active` referenced downstream: the AST parsed, and it would have `NameError`d at runtime. Removing test methods by `def` anchor orphaned their `@patch` decorators, surfacing only as an `IndentationError` two classes later. Syntax, AST, import and behavioural checks each caught a different one; none of them would have caught all three.
  - **The vPR queue is deliberately kept**, diverging from `migration.md`'s recommendation to retire it alongside the rest. The bare-mirror SOP covers a forge *outage* (a procedure a human invokes); the vPR queue covers *transient* unreachability inside a single `auto-pr` run (queue locally, flush later, automatically). Those are different needs, and the queue is live machinery whose three failure modes were repaired the same week (WI-buniz).
  - **Governance surface updated with it:** `AGENTS.md` no longer lists `ci-failover` as an approved network-calling script or a governance file, its pre-work checklist no longer instructs agents to prefer a `selfh` remote, and its secrets line no longer describes the GitHub backend as "dormant" — that has been backwards since the migration. `CODEOWNERS` drops the deleted path, `.env.template` drops the `SELFHOSTED_FORGEJO_*` block, and the pre-work playbook keeps the 2026-04-01 incident that motivated the authoritative-remote check while recording that the class of error is now gone by construction rather than by vigilance.

### Fixed
- **Two taint-propagator defects that silently lost real findings — one of them hiding a violated security claim.** Both sat on the path `verify-claims` runs Python through, and both failed in the direction that *loses* findings, which for a security tool is the expensive error: a suppressed flow is indistinguishable from a clean repo.
  - **The DDG pass kept a private, ungated copy of sanitizer registration (INV-finoh, second site).** `propagate_taint_ddg` matched sanitizers on bare short name with no `is_resolved`, `call_construct` or `ambiguous_names` filter. Because a barrier *prunes* the walk, that copy did not merely over-register — an unrelated unresolved `x.encrypt()` bound `Fernet.encrypt` and **deleted a real flow**. INV-finoh's own `root_cause` and `fix` text named **two** sites, "the helper and its DDG mirror"; the fix landed at the helper, and its closure evidence was gathered on `propagate_taint_structural` — not the function Python routes through (L53: name the instrument, then verify it is the one production runs). Now routed through the shared `_register_sanitizer_callers`, so the two cannot drift again. Repro pinned in tests: identical four-node graph, one unresolved bare method call added on an **intermediate** hop (a collision on the source function does not reproduce it — the seed is exempt from the barrier check by design) takes the DDG pass from 1 finding to 0 while structural holds at 1.
  - **`sink_callers` was a dict keyed on the calling symbol, so a function calling two sinks reported one.** Populated by assignment, each sink overwrote the last; present in **both** passes. Now a list per caller consumed through a shared `_iter_sink_sites`, so neither loop body changed and the two passes share one implementation.
  - **Measured on the 9-repo cohort, instrument asserted in both directions first** (`OK both_fixes_live` on the fix, `BAD:sanitizer_gate_unfixed,sink_overwrite_structural,sink_overwrite_ddg` against the prior tree): flows **149 → 193** (+47, caddy 34 → 68 where `printEnvironment` calls both `Printf` and `Println` and only one was reported; kserve 13 → 18; alertmanager 14 → 15). **One claim verdict changed: `pretix/host-secret-no-network` `confirmed` → `violated`** — a claim reported as *satisfied* while it was being violated. The three apparent removals in pretix are **not** removals: its `untrusted-input-no-database` verdict sits at exactly 100 evidence rows in both arms, an output cap over ~741 flows, so added findings displace which 100 are shown. Neither fix can remove a flow.
- **ADR-0017's precision half is documented but does not run — six artifacts, each closed `done` on "N tests, 100% coverage".** `_sink_module_compatible` (retired last release) was the first found member of a family, not an isolated case. Measured 2026-08-02: **§3a DDG propagation** builds its forward index `ddg_forward` and its tainted-variable set `tainted_at` and reads neither, so DDG data selects only a confidence *label* and has never influenced which flows are reported, for any language, since March 2026; **§4a/§4b function summaries** (`infer_summary`, `load_function_summaries`) have zero production callers; **§7a field-sensitivity** (`is_field_tainted`) has zero production callers; the **Rust and TypeScript def/use extractors** have zero production callers *and* their CFG mappings lack `atomic_statement`, so they would emit zero DDG edges even if invoked; the **§3c mixed-coverage verdict** is never emitted, because `TaintFlowFinding.confidence` reaches no consumer (`verify_claims` hardcodes the string `"approximate"` and the evidence dict omits the field). A second, independent blocker under §3a: `DdgEdge.def_block` is a **function-local** block id (`bb_5` recurs in every function — 306 distinct values across 1,306 functions) compared against a *symbol* id, so that branch can never fire.
  - **The documents said otherwise and are corrected in place.** ADR-0017's phase table opened with *"All originally-planned phases have shipped"* — deleted, and the table gains a **Runs?** column stating per phase whether it affects output. The `Status:` line now discloses the four unimplemented sections, because `docs/adr/README.md` requires that *a grep of the status line must not lie*. §3a's numbered algorithm is marked **NOT IMPLEMENTED — target design** at its head rather than in a trailing note, since a fragment read of the steps would otherwise be indistinguishable from a description of the code; the correction also records that step 5 is underspecified in the load-bearing way (*"if tainted data reaches a sink"* must mean *a tainted variable is an argument at the sink call site*). The spec's claims that DDG-backed tracking is live "currently Python, Rust, TypeScript", that it "eliminates false positives", that the solver "consults a function summary", and that field-sensitivity applies are each marked specified-not-implemented. **Note the recurrence this closes:** last release's `_sink_module_compatible` correction edited the very paragraph containing *"→ DDG-backed propagation with module filtering"* and left that phrase standing — the correction pass reproduced the defect it was correcting.

- **`_sink_module_compatible` is retired — ADR-specified, described as live by four docstrings, and never called.** Added 2026-05-14 with **zero production callers** — its only reachability was six unit tests, which is exactly what held it at 100% coverage and kept it invisible. Meanwhile the contract it was written for was implemented by a *different* function (`_lookup_named_entry` → `io_boundary._module_matches`) with different internals, and the two drifted: the live one was bidirectional *substring* containment until WI-zazul and ungated resolved edges entirely until WI-damir.
  - **Harvested before deleting.** The dead function was **more correct** on one point: it exempted both `external` *and* `<external>` from module filtering, per ADR-0017 §3a, while the live path tested only the bare spelling. So an `<external>` hint fell into the module-*filter* branch and was compared as though it were a real module name — matching nothing and **suppressing** the finding, the opposite of the ADR's "degrade to short-name matching, because rejecting outright would suppress legitimate findings". `<external>` is not hypothetical: the pretix cohort emits `javascript:<external>:0-0:file:external_symbol` for receivers no DDG resolution can recover. Both spellings now live in one `_UNRESOLVED_MODULE_PLACEHOLDERS` frozenset, which is what stops the pair drifting apart again. **Measured on the 9-repo cohort: zero change** (272 flows / 13 non-realizable, identical, 0 added and 0 removed) — the instrument check confirms the exemption is active, so this is a real null rather than a stale read: `<external>` occurs there as flow *seed symbols* rather than as callee module hints reaching the matcher. The change is justified by ADR-0017 §3a conformance and pinned by a unit test; its practical impact on real corpora is **unmeasured, not zero**, and it is the only permissive-direction change in this series so it can only ever add flows.
  - **Four docstrings described a mechanism that does not run**, including `taint_refine.py`'s **module** docstring — that pass's stated reason for existing was *"the rewritten dst then participates in `_sink_module_compatible` directly"*. The pass has a real effect, through a different consumer with different semantics, so anyone reading it to learn what module strings must look like reasoned about the wrong predicate. L50 at the largest scale it has appeared: not a comment justifying a boundary, but a module's stated purpose. All four corrected to name the live filter.
  - **ADR-0017 amended in place at three sites** (per `docs/adr/README.md` — ADRs are edited in place, git history is the record): §3a now names the real filter and carries a dated correction; the per-language section no longer attributes the work to the dead function; and §"Production deployments" no longer claims hypergumbo's own self-audit runs "sink-module-compatibility filtering", which was simply false.
  - **The real ADR-0037 debt is now filed rather than implied.** Both invalidation markers say *"implementing fixes will re-key sink matching and refinement on `dst_ref`"* — `dst_ref` appears **14 times in `ir.py` and zero times in `taint.py`**. Sink matching still string-parses `dst`, which is what forces `_extract_callee_module` to return a module for external symbols and a *file path* for in-repo ones with nothing in its name or signature to say which — the ambiguity WI-damir's defect rode in on.
  - **Dogfooding, because the tool exists to catch exactly this.** `hypergumbo dead-code-maybe` on its own tree **does** flag `_sink_module_compatible`, vindicating D7's production-seed default: the function is reachable only from tests, and 100% line coverage concealed it entirely. But the finding is unusable as delivered — `test_only_reachable: 1851` of 2094 candidates is reported as a **summary count only**, with no per-candidate cohort label in either `--format json` or `--format text`, and the list ranked by LOC. The tool computed the discriminator and dropped it on the way out. Filed as its own item, and noted there as the second instance this session of one shape: *correct computation, lossy emission, unverifiable by the consumer* — the other being the 13 residual `net/http`+`Do` matches.
- **A *resolved* first-party symbol matched a catalog primitive by name alone — caddy's `func Log()` became a logging sink 18 times, and d3's logarithm became `console.log` (WI-damir).** `_match_propagation_entry` short-circuited every resolved edge to an ungated `return hits[0]`, justified in a comment as *"the symbol is already disambiguated by resolution."* That premise is false: resolution establishes **which in-repo symbol is called**, not whether that symbol **is** the catalogued external primitive. The catalogs describe stdlib and third-party surfaces, so matching a first-party definition against one by name is a category error — measured **30 of 30 false** on the 9-repo fresh-substrate cohort. The third instance today of L50, a boundary-justifying comment stating a false fact and foreclosing the option it declares unnecessary.
  - **The gate cannot be "resolved edges never match"** — a user-supplied catalog may legitimately name a first-party symbol, which is what the old comment was protecting. So the entry's declared module is compared against the symbol's own **path**, normalised to module shape. This works only because of the component-**suffix** arm added for WI-zazul: `hypergumbo_core.cli` matches a path of `packages/…/hypergumbo_core/cli.py`, while `log/slog` does not match `logging.go`. A test pins the first-party-catalog case so the fix cannot degenerate into "reject everything resolved".
  - **Measured on the same cohort, same checkouts, same claims file, with an instrument check on each arm.** Non-realizable sink matches **43 → 13**; flows 312 → 272. The 13 survivors are all `net/http`+`Do`, where the match is correct and only the *label* is lossy (the emitted module records the package, the catalog records `net/http.Client`). Every one of the 23 removed flows was verified against the source rather than counted: **14** first-party sinks (caddy's `Log`, which *returns* a logger and writes nothing; d3's `scaleLog`; `iframeResizer` `warn`; express's `debug`), **6** where the *source* was fabricated — caddy's own `Run(cfg)` matched to **`gin.Engine.Run`**, a framework caddy does not use — and **3** genuine flows through a first-party thin wrapper (`def run(cmd): return subprocess.run(...).stdout`), filed as its own item rather than papered over by loosening the gate. Restoring 3 real flows by readmitting 20 false ones would be a bad trade.
  - **A length heuristic for "file extension" was wrong and an existing test caught it in seconds.** The first draft stripped "a short alphanumeric segment after a dot", which rewrote the real module `net.ws` to `net`. No threshold can separate an extension from a short module component; the set is now named explicitly, with a test pinning `net.ws` and `os.environ` against `logging.go` and `d3.v6.js`. That is the fourth time in this arc that a **pre-existing** test caught a design error — the others being the Go slash-vs-dot assumption and the unqualified-`System` suffix case.
  - **Also noted:** `_sink_module_compatible` exists in `taint.py` with zero callers — written for approximately this purpose and never wired in. Its dotted-prefix rule would have rejected the legitimate first-party case, which is likely why.
- **Taint and I/O sink matching used bidirectional SUBSTRING containment, so `os` matched `chaos` and `net/http` matched `net/http/fcgi` (WI-zazul).** `io_boundary._module_matches` normalised `::` and `/` to `.` and then asked `cm in em or em in cm`. Twenty-five of the 210 catalog sink modules are four characters or fewer (`os`, `io`, `fs`, `net`, `log`, `sys`, `rpc`, `ssl` …), so each matched any module whose normalised path merely *contained* it. Found by the 2026-08-01 taint cohort over 9 fresh external repos — the run that moved INV-karud to `violated` — which produced d3's `log` (**the logarithm function**) reported as a logging sink and `net/http/httptest.NewRequest`, the *test* request constructor that performs no network IO, reported as a network sink.
  - **Matching is now component-aware**, with a different rule per direction and the safety property stated for each. Exact or component-prefix match, where a *strict* prefix is decided by the first extra component: capitalised means a type inside the matched module (`os/exec` + `Cmd`), lowercase means a sibling or sub-package (`net/http` + `fcgi`, `httptest`, `smtp`) and must not match. Component-**suffix** match for dropped qualification, because that is how source actually spells these — Go writes `http.Get` after importing `net/http`, Java writes `System.in` for `java.lang.System.in` — and leading namespace cannot turn one module into another the way an appended sub-package can.
  - **The obvious fix does not work, and an existing test said so.** Keeping `/` distinct from `.` cleanly separates `net/http.Client` (a type) from `net/http/fcgi` (a package) — except the Go analyzer emits `os.exec.Cmd` for what the catalog spells `os/exec`, so the separator is not evidence of a package boundary. Go's naming convention is: package names lowercase, exported types capitalised. A second existing test then caught an over-tightening — `java.lang.System` against a hint of `System` is a component *suffix*, not a prefix. Both regressions were caught by tests that predate this change, which is why the whole io-boundary and taint suite was run rather than only the new class.
  - **The Swift carve-out's DIRECTION is its safety property.** Swift hints are receiver *variable* names (camelCase) against PascalCase catalog types, and the variable is often the type's trailing word, so component matching cannot express `fileIO` → `NonBlockingFileIO`. The carve-out therefore allows the catalog name to end with the hint and never the reverse: `'chaos'.endswith('os')` is the bug, `'NonBlockingFileIO'.endswith('fileIO')` is the feature. It is further bounded to suffixes starting on a capital, so it cannot degenerate back into substring containment for short names.
  - **Impact measured, not assumed.** The item's own filed repro re-runs clean: all five verified-live collisions, all four fresh-substrate observations, and the one catalog-internal pair that flips a *zone* rather than a primitive (`sys.process.Process` is host_fs, `sys.process.ProcessBuilder` is subprocess, and the former is literally a substring of the latter). Self-tree impact is **nil**: an A/B with the analysis cache cleared and a positive control on each arm — asserting the loaded predicate's verdict on `os`/`chaos` before measuring — gives 735 boundary calls both ways with a **byte-identical** whole output. That null is expected rather than suspicious: every observed defect was Go or JavaScript, and this tree is Python, which is exactly why INV-karud refused to close on self-tree numbers. The self-tree validation ratchet is clean, so the shrink-only re-baseline the item anticipated was not needed.
  - **A first attempt at that A/B was discarded as untrustworthy.** It compared 735 against 735 with its only validity check a grep for `cm in em or em in cm` — a string present in both the old *code* and the new *docstring* that quotes it, so it could not distinguish the arms. Two identical numbers from an instrument that cannot tell the conditions apart is a stale read, not a null result; the rerun proves which predicate is loaded on each arm before measuring.
  - **Known tradeoff, recorded in the docstring rather than left to be discovered:** a lowercase extra component now blocks a match even in languages that do not signal types by case, so this can under-match where it previously over-matched. For a sink catalog that is the safe direction — a missed sink is a gap, a spurious one is a false claim about the program's behaviour.
- **A PR could merge while CI was still pending: the hung-runner check was structurally unsatisfiable on Woodpecker, and four merge gates failed OPEN on its verdict (WI-ninar + WI-ditav).** These ship together because neither is safe alone — fixing only the gates makes every pipeline over 300 s refuse to merge, and fixing only the check leaves the fail-open path live for every other verdict. Observed live: PR #160 merged with `ci/woodpecker/pr/woodpecker` still `pending`.
  - **`poll_ci` asked a question its backend can never answer `yes` to (WI-ninar).** It decided "has any job started?" by testing whether any status context had gone **non-pending** — correct for Forgejo Actions, which posted one context *per job*, so contexts flipped as individual jobs finished. Woodpecker posts exactly **one aggregate context** that stays `pending` for the entire pipeline. The answer was therefore "no" for the whole run, and every pipeline outliving `CI_STALE_PENDING_SECONDS` (300 s default, against a ~360 s suite) was declared a hung runner. It was not detecting anything. The predicate now asks whether a context **exists at all** — Woodpecker posts it when it creates the run, so its presence proves dispatch and its absence is the dispatch failure the check was written for. `any_job_started` is renamed `run_dispatched`, and a parse failure now keeps polling rather than fabricating a hung verdict, so an unreadable backend surfaces as an honest timeout. The threshold is deliberately **not** raised — that was the by-hand workaround that forced #160 through, and it moves the false verdict rather than removing it.
  - **Four consumers enumerated the codes that BLOCK, so exit 3 was a permit (WI-ditav).** The filing named one site; the audit found four. `merge-pr` and auto-pr's **post-rebase merge path** fell through to the merge on exit 3 *as shipped*; auto-pr's flush and main paths would fall through on any code added later. Exit 3 was introduced after those guards were written, by a different fix — so a guard correct on the day it was authored silently became permission. Notably `poll_ci`'s own header comment documented only "0 = success, 1 = failure, 2 = timeout" and never mentioned 3, which is plausibly how four call sites came to omit it. **The two sites that were already safe were safe for exactly the reason L54 gives** — they branch on `rc == 0` and treat everything else as refusal — which is the argument for making that the only available spelling.
  - **The fix is a chokepoint, not "also handle 3"** — that reproduces the same shape one code later. `ci_verdict_permits_merge` in the forge library is now the single home for "may we merge on this verdict?": it returns 0 only for 0, preserves the code as its own exit status, and names an unrecognised verdict explicitly rather than refusing silently. Per-site **recovery text stays at the call sites**, because the flush, main and post-rebase paths legitimately advise different next steps — the chokepoint owns the field that must be uniform and leaves the ones that legitimately differ to callers (L27). Retry-loop conditions (`-eq 2`, `-eq 3` guarding *"should we try again?"*) are untouched; that is a different question from *"may we proceed?"* and enumerating there is correct.
  - **Behavioural tests, no forge required.** `poll_ci` is driven with `api_get` overridden in the sourced shell, so the WI-ninar change is distinguished from the bug it replaces on identical input: one pending aggregate context past the threshold must **not** yield exit 3, while an empty status list still must — without that second assertion the fix would be indistinguishable from deleting the check. Mutation-tested three ways: restoring the old predicate, making unknown verdicts permit, and re-hand-rolling merge-pr's gate each turn exactly the corresponding test red.
  - **Branch protection was not the backstop it was assumed to be.** The required check `ci/woodpecker/pr/woodpecker` was configured correctly on both `dev` and `main`, but **"Do not allow bypassing the above settings" was unticked**, so the rules did not apply to the merging account — which is the only way a merge succeeds against a pending required check. Now ticked on both. Recorded because the *assumption* that protection would catch this was stated an hour before the path fired, and it was wrong.
  - **Per-PR coverage closed rather than disclosed.** The new test's subject is `scripts/lib/*`, which the top-level test mapper deliberately does not map — so instead of only exempting it, it is named in the `forge-arms` step of `.woodpecker/woodpecker.yml`, which runs on any `scripts/**` change. Editing the forge library, `merge-pr` or `auto-pr` therefore does execute it before merge.
- **Post-merge push pipelines reported green having run zero tests: the gate failed OPEN on a git error, and its diff base was a commit that no longer existed (WI-vilor).** Caught on the dev push pipeline for the WI-fodad merge itself, which printed `fatal: bad object 66737979de...` immediately followed by `✅ PR changes no .py files — skipping pytest` and then reported **success**, on a commit that changed nine files including four Python ones. Two defects compounding:
  - **A failed `git diff` is byte-identical to a clean one.** The condition was `[ -z "$(git diff --name-only "$BASE" "$HEAD" | grep -E '\.py$' || true)" ]`; a diff that *errors* writes nothing to stdout, so the gate could not distinguish "nothing changed" from "I could not find out" and resolved the ambiguity by skipping the suite. It now captures the diff and its exit status separately and skips **only** on a successful diff that genuinely reported no `.py` — L54's default-deny shape, applied to the same shell block WI-modur repaired for a different hole.
  - **`CI_PREV_COMMIT_SHA` is the previous *pipeline's* commit, not the previous tip of the branch.** `auto-pr` merges by **rebase**, which rewrites the commit, so after every rebase merge that SHA names an object absent from the post-merge clone — verified for this run: `66737979de` was the pre-rebase copy of `6e92792a34`, reachable only from the feature branch `auto-pr` had just deleted, and not an ancestor of `dev`. This fired on **every** rebase-merged push, not intermittently. The base is now validated with `git cat-file -e` before use and falls back to `HEAD^`; the fallback is deliberately scoped rather than unconditional, since always diffing `HEAD^` undercounts a push landing more than one commit.
  - **Mutation-tested both ways** in `tests/test_ci_pytest_selection_gate.py`, which executes the extracted shell block against stub binaries rather than asserting on its text: reinstating either the one-line diff or the unvalidated base turns exactly the corresponding new test red. This is the fifth distinct route in the L48 family and the second inside this one block — which is the argument for the declarative `covers:` marker the top-level test map keeps asking for.
- **Per-PR CI skipped pytest entirely unless a `packages/*/src/**.py` file changed — whole classes of PR ran no tests and reported green (WI-modur).** The gate decided "should pytest run?" from the set of changed *source* files, so a test-only fix, a `scripts/*.py` change, an `.agent/hooks/**.py` change, or any mix with docs/CI produced an empty source set and exited 0. Caught on the WI-fodad PR, whose committed manifest carried **0 changed source files and 1 selected test**: `smart-test` had correctly selected the changed test and CI threw the answer away. The gate now skips only when the manifest's `SELECTED_TESTS` section is empty — the question it actually means to ask — while changed-source files continue to size the coverage target, which legitimately needs them. This is L48's family one level up: not "a test that cannot be selected is indistinguishable from a test that passes", but *an entire class of pull request for which the suite never runs*, reported as a pass.
  - **Pinned by tests that execute the gate rather than read it.** The shell block is extracted from the YAML and run against a fabricated manifest with stub binaries on a **replaced** `PATH` (L49), asserting on whether pytest was actually invoked. A source-text assertion would have been worthless — the old broken condition also mentioned the manifest. **Mutation-tested:** reintroducing the original condition turns 3 of the 4 tests red.
- **CI cloned without tree objects, so the suite's one history-wide git walk re-fetched ~26,000 trees from origin one at a time (WI-fodad).** `tests/test_backfill_training_data_cohort_tags.py` intermittently failed five tests at once on `subprocess.TimeoutExpired`, all from a single `git log --pretty=format:'%aI %H' --reverse -- <one path>`. **Measured in CI by a purpose-built probe: 1,185,559 ms on the first invocation and 19 ms on the second**, against **27 ms** on a dev box. The cause was the *checkout*, not the command. `woodpeckerci/plugin-git` defaults `partial: true`, and its own `defaults.go` then sets `Depth = 1` and `filter = "tree:0"`, so the workspace was a **promisor repository holding no tree objects** beyond the single commit it checked out. `git fetch --unshallow` in `prepare-git` restores the commit history but **inherits the configured filter**, so the trees stayed missing. Commit-only reads never noticed — `dco`'s `rev-list`, `merge-base`, the recency window — but a pathspec walk needs a tree from every commit, and each one became a separate HTTPS round trip. All three workflows now pin `settings: {partial: false}`; the trade is one ~264 MB bulk clone against ~26,000 round trips.
  - **Why the 44,000x could not have been what it was repeatedly diagnosed as.** The workspace is a local ext4 SSD (`/dev/sda2`), which rules out disk latency arithmetically, and CI ran the whole suite *faster* than the dev box (359 s vs 572 s), which rules out contention. The decisive datum was the 19 ms second run: that is a fresh git process, so nothing in-process was cached, and page-cache warming cannot turn 1,185 s into 19 ms for a 264 MB pack set. Something had to be *arriving* on the first run. It also explains the intermittency — the agent reuses the workspace across pipelines, and a warm workspace already holds the fetched trees.
  - **Two earlier attempts were the same bug, and one of them triggered it deliberately.** A `git commit-graph write --reachable --changed-paths` warm-up in `prepare-git` took CI from ~359 s to ~15 minutes: `--changed-paths` must diff every commit, so it demanded the identical ~26,000 trees, on *every* pipeline, to serve one rarely-selected test. Reverted. Raising the subprocess cap from 30 s to 1800 s then let the walk run to completion and pushed the pytest step past the agent's task deadline, so the pipeline was **cancelled** (`queue: task expired`) — trading a fast, legible red for an unattributable infrastructure error. The cap is now **120 s**: roughly 4,000x headroom over the real cost, with its only job being to stop a genuinely wedged git.
  - **Guarded twice, because there are two distinct failure modes.** `tests/test_ci_clone_is_complete.py` asserts statically that every `.woodpecker/*.yml` clone step declares `partial: false` — absent is the bug, since the plugin's default is `true` — handling both of Woodpecker's accepted `clone:` spellings and carrying a non-vacuity floor. Separately, `prepare-git` reads `remote.origin.partialclonefilter` at run time and fails the pipeline, because no static check can see the declaration silently ceasing to take effect after a plugin bump or a server-side clone override. Both are **default-deny** (L54): they require the safe value to be stated rather than enumerating the filters known to hurt. Mutation-tested — removing either half turns the corresponding test red.
  - **Nine hypotheses died before the cause was found, and the record is kept because the pattern is the point.** Runner starvation, shallow history, missing commit-graph, over-parallelism, fresh-clone and shallow-then-unshallow repo states, a `gitattributes` textconv driver, background auto-gc, and cold pack I/O — each eliminated by measurement rather than argument. Every local reproduction attempt was invalid for one shared reason: a locally-made shallow clone carries **no filter**, so it has all its trees. The answer came from reading the vendor's `defaults.go`, not from another probe.
- **The subprocess→CLI linker emitted zero join edges on any monorepo, and a green test said otherwise (WI-gadus).** hypergumbo's own tree contains both halves of the construct the WI-lubap fix targets — 26 argparse `add_parser`/`set_defaults(func=)` pairs in `cli.py`, and `scripts/hypergumbo_diag.py:960` invoking `subprocess.run(["hypergumbo", "slice", "--input", in_path, ...])` — yet a full survey produced **0** `subprocess_calls` edges against 41,448 nodes / 140,086 edges while minting 90 call-site symbols. The WI-lubap join itself was never at fault: measured against the real symbol set it resolves 26/26 subcommands to 27 handler symbols. Three defects sat upstream of it, the first two each sufficient alone to force the zero.
  - **CLI-name detection read one file at one path.** `_detect_project_cli_name` consulted only `<repo_root>/pyproject.toml`, so any workspace whose root manifest is tool-config-only was structurally dark — including this one, whose root has no `[project]` table at all while the `hypergumbo` console script is declared in `packages/hypergumbo/pyproject.toml`. It returned an empty set, so the `call.executable in project_cli_names` guard could never pass for any call in the repository and every mechanism behind it was dead code. Now unions `[project.name]`, `[project.scripts]`, `setup(name=)` and `entry_points={"console_scripts": [...]}` across every `pyproject.toml` and `setup.py` the shared discovery excludes admit; `setup.py` is read via `ast` (located structurally, literal keywords only) rather than executed, so a computed name fails safe to contributing nothing. Deliberately over-selects: a vendored package named after a common binary costs one spurious edge, where the old behavior cost every real one. `setup.cfg` remains unread — a declared gap, not a claim.
  - **One non-literal argv element discarded the whole call site.** `_extract_command_info` ran `ast.literal_eval` over the entire captured list, and real self-invocations are almost never fully literal — the diag call carries five variables after the subcommand, so it raised `ValueError` and the site was dropped despite `SUBPROCESS_CALL_PATTERN` matching it. Now parses element-wise and keeps the leading run of constants, which is exactly where the executable and subcommand live. Stopping at the first non-constant rather than skipping past it is deliberate: once an element is opaque, positional reasoning about what follows is unsound. `python -m <var>` fails safe to no command rather than reporting `python` as the executable. The swallowing branch had carried a `# pragma: no cover` while demonstrably executing on this project's own source; it is now covered by a test instead.
  - **Handler lookup preferred an `<external>` placeholder over the real function.** `_scan_argparse_commands` indexed handlers by bare name with no kind or path filter, unlike `_scan_fire_commands`. On this tree the subcommand `survey` resolved to both the real `cmd_run` and a same-named `external_symbol` whose `<external>` path sentinel sorts before every real path — so the INV-zuhub `min(candidates, key=id)` tie-break actively selected the placeholder, and fixing the first two defects alone would have minted a confidently-wrong edge at confidence 0.5. External placeholders are now excluded outright, and a handler defined in the same file as its `add_parser`/`set_defaults` pair wins over any same-named symbol elsewhere. Unlike the fire linker this falls back to a name-only match rather than requiring same-file, because `from .commands import cmd_serve` is common enough that requiring it would trade one wrong-edge class for a recall hole.
  - **Measured end-to-end on this repository, which is the only instrument that can see this defect: 0 → 4 `subprocess_calls` edges.** Two are genuine self-invocations that the old code discarded, both for the same reason (a variable in the argv): `scripts/hypergumbo_diag.py:960 → cmd_slice` at confidence 0.85 — the join WI-lubap was filed for — and `scripts/dead-code-prospector-run.py:520 → cmd_dead_code_maybe`. The other two are `subprocess.run([...])` written inside *string literals* in `test_subprocess_linker.py`; the scanner is regex-over-raw-text with no string-literal awareness, which is a pre-existing limitation now visible because the executable guard finally passes. It is recorded on WI-gadus as a decision rather than folded in here, since suppressing it means teaching the scanner about literals.
  - **Testing.** The pre-existing `test_links_subprocess_to_argparse_handler` has been green since July while the feature was dead in production, because its fixture defeats all three blockers by construction — a root `pyproject.toml` *with* a `[project]` table, a fully literal argv, and exactly one candidate symbol. The new suite is the same scenario with each convenience removed (nested-package console script; argv carrying variables; a same-named external placeholder competing with the real handler), plus an end-to-end case with all three at once — 6 red-first tests, 6 more pinning the fail-safe branches, module at 100%. The unit suite alone would not have caught this: it was the whole-repository probe that produced the zero, and it is the probe — not a cohort — that validates the fix.

### Added
- **WI-hafap Stage 2 complete: all 48 `Optional[Span]` dereference errors drained per-site (never a sweep) — mypy strict surface 720 → 672, exactly the 48 targeted with zero reveals.** Every one of the 31 distinct sites got an individually-decided fix shape (each proposal adversarially verified against the 11 `span=None`-constructing test files, mypy narrowing semantics, statement coverage, and production behavior): line-0 ternaries at edge-emission sites (grpc ×2, solidity overrides, slice's disambiguation error, the `Symbol.line`/`end_line` compat properties); filter-out guards at position-index sites where a fabricated `(path, 0)` key would be a bogus join entry (message_queue, event_sourcing, js_ts, java, dart, asm, sparql, solidity); `(Symbol, Span)` tuple-carrying restructures in the router/controller route linkers so the entry filter's narrowing survives the collection boundary in the type system (zero runtime re-checks); a paired-`end_line` restructure in `compute_truncation_elbow`; zero-Span-anchored route minting in `framework_patterns` (`make_route_symbol` requires a concrete Span); narrow-locals in `analyze/base`'s occurrence sort keys; and 2 asserts where construction with a real span is visible in the same function (thrift, proto). Behavior is byte-identical for every previously-constructible input — pinned by 9 new tests including the caddy/kafka-style line-zero pins.
- **The `Symbol.span` honest-carry chokepoint (WI-hafap option 3, executed).** `to_dict` now serializes a span-less Symbol as an explicit schema-legal `null` (never a fabricated zero-Span dict), and `from_dict` deserializes a missing **or** null span to `None` instead of fabricating the degenerate `Span(0,0,0,0)` that passed every truthiness guard while claiming to occupy line 0 — the exact hole the original WI-hafap filing named. Round-trip pinned (`span=None → null → None`); no production emitter constructs a span-less Symbol, so the change is observable only for legacy/corrupt artifacts. All 13 raw-dict `get("span", {})` readers in `cli.py` converted to the in-file null-tolerant `(x.get("span") or {})` convention — 2 sites already used it, so 15 now carry it and 0 old-shape readers remain; the 2 bare pass-through emissions that never dereference are deliberately untouched.
- **Decoded `node.text` has one home: `analyze.base.node_own_text` (WI-sarag; the WI-vokiz None-guard baked in at the chokepoint).** The filing counted 14 byte-identical private `_get_node_text` copies; the sweep found **35** — 18 guarding `node.text`'s None with `if node.text else ""`, 14 with `(node.text or b"")`, and assorted drift — the half-guarded-population tell (no contract, only local habit; the same shape `Symbol.span` had). 34 copies spliced (AST-anchored, per-file asserts per the L52 discipline) into an aliased import of the single None-safe helper, so every historical call site is untouched while the fact lives once; css.py's `(node, source)` source-slice variant deliberately stays — it matches the *other* contract (`node_text(node, source)`, for re-parsed sub-trees whose `.text` is unavailable) and the recurrence test exempts it by signature. A recurrence linter (`test_node_own_text.py`) fails the build on any new private copy, mirroring `member_names`' enforcement shape. Verified: 2235 tests green across both swept packages, all packages at 100% coverage in isolation, mypy ratchet delta +0.
- **auto-pr's vPR machinery stops minting phantom queue entries and can always drain (WI-buniz — three failure modes, all observed live while landing PR #144).** (1) **Rejection is not unavailability:** a push rejected non-fast-forward (the remote branch already held the change from auto-pr's own hung-run re-push cycle) was reported "remote unavailable" and queued a duplicate vPR while the real PR sat open. The classifier now distinguishes: remote tip already at the local sha → the push is done, proceed; genuinely diverged → a loud NOT-queueing error (return 2, handled distinctly at both the do_pr and flush call sites). (2) **Staleness is content identity, not sha identity:** flush *rebuilds* commits, so a merged vPR's recorded sha never appears in the base branch and the ancestor-only `prune` called it live forever — measured: the queued commit differed from the merged one solely in `.ci/affected-tests.txt`'s timestamp header. The predicate adds two content checks with that manifest excluded (tree-identity with the base tip, then a bounded filtered-patch-id scan over the last 100 base commits), with an explicit carve-out: an *empty* commit can't be judged by content (its tree trivially equals whatever it sits on), so ancestry stays its only stale signal. (3) **A dead entry must be prunable, loudly:** an unresolvable sha (branch deleted post-merge) made `flush` die on git's raw `Needed a single revision` and left prune calling the entry live; reconciliation now carries a third BROKEN class that prune drops with an explicit unverifiable-content warning and flush pre-diagnoses with a pointer at prune. New `tests/test_autopr_vpr_reconcile.py` drives the real script through all three shapes (seam gains `reject-same`/`reject-diverged`); 92 tests green across the seven auto-pr suites.
- **`ci-debug logs` no longer crashes on a Woodpecker 200-with-empty-body (WI-holik).** The log endpoint can return HTTP 200 whose body is JSON `null` (or an error-envelope object); `sorted(None)` raised a bare `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not iterable` — a traceback at the exact moment the operator needs the failed-CI log (observed live while diagnosing PR #144's errored run). The parse now diagnoses the shape (`returned NoneType, not a log-entry list (an error response with a 200 code)`) and degrades to the documented fallback. Tests drive the REAL `_github_fetch_job_log` end-to-end through an upgraded fake curl that now emulates the no-`-o` + `-w '\n%{http_code}'` shape (body on stdout, code appended) — the Woodpecker calls were previously untestable because the fake only modeled the `-o` form; the happy-path test proves the base64 line-decode still works through the same harness.
- **`Symbol.span` is now `Optional[Span]` — the declaration matches reality, and the mypy ratchet drops 735 → 720 (WI-hafap Stage 1, INV-zogud drain).** The old non-Optional declaration was load-bearing ambiguity in its worst form: ten test files construct `Symbol(span=None)` and Python doesn't enforce annotations, so the checker's verdicts were laundered on every span site — 52 live truthiness guards read "always true" (truthy-bool), 7 is-None guard bodies read "unreachable", 5 span expressions read "redundant", and a clean-ratchet rewrite once deleted 43 live guards on that basis (832→789, caught only by the test suite).
