The flip removes all 64 of those false verdicts and **surfaces 48 real ones the lie was hiding**: 42 union-attr (unguarded `sym.span.<attr>` dereferences) and 6 arg-type (call sites now visibly passing `Span | None` into `Span`-typed parameters). Per-code, measured: truthy-bool 66→14, unreachable 30→23, redundant-expr 20→15, union-attr 29→71, arg-type 104→111 (one of the +7 is pre-existing drift, see below). Stage 2 drains the 48 surfaced sites **per-site** (guard where a span-less symbol can reach the site, assert where the caller established presence) in small reviewed batches — never a blanket sweep; the 43-deleted-guards episode is the standing proof of why. Baseline regenerated via `check-mypy-ratchet --update-baseline`; strict mode verifies delta +0 against the new baseline.
  - **Baseline drift reconciled in the same PR rather than laundered into the flip's numbers:** live dev measured 740 against the committed 735 — +4 `attr-defined` ("AST has no attribute lineno" at `symbol_kinds.py`'s member-literal linter, introduced by e98bc23385 after the baseline commit) and +1 `arg-type`. The four lineno errors are **fixed** (narrow via `getattr` at the walk-loop head — `ast.walk` yields bare `AST`, which typeshed gives no `lineno`; every node yielding member literals has one at runtime), returning `attr-defined` to its baseline 3. The stray arg-type rides in the 104→111 cell, itemized here so the ledger stays honest.
- **`compact --max-symbols` containment monotonicity restored (WI-vofud, the WI-kolal regression).** Measured 2026-08-01 on the eight 07-17 bakeoff maps: `nodes(K1) ⊆ nodes(K2)` was violated on **6 of 8 repos**, deterministically (caddy 50→100 dropped its `Dispenser` dispatch endpoints; zoxide and spring-petclinic violated at two consecutive budget steps; sherpa-csharp at every step). Three budget-dependent mechanisms, all in the seed machinery — which the prior containment test never exercised (its fixture had `entrypoints: []` and force-include off): the entrypoint cap **switched regimes** on `len(eps) > max_symbols` (1/3 vs 1/2), the cross-cutting seed budget (`max//2 − len(forced)`) **shrank as the forced set grew** (at a larger budget the cross-cutting seeds could vanish entirely), and the coverage stop **counted seed centrality**, so a bigger seed set stopped the centrality fill earlier. The monotone replacement, default path only: ONE fixed-order seed list (entrypoints by `-confidence`, then cross-cutting endpoints by `-edge_count`) under ONE quota that can only grow with the budget (`max//2`, floor-of-one reserved for entrypoint seeds), plus a **budget-independent positional stop** in `select_by_coverage` computed from the global centrality order alone — a prefix of a fixed list under a monotone quota is nested by construction. Connectivity mode (`--connectivity`) deliberately keeps the old adaptive policy: the aggressive 1/3 cap exists for its bridging use-case (keycloak fragmentation) and bridge expansion is not a ranked prefix, so budget-containment was never that mode's contract. Verified: the same real-map sweep now reads **8/8 OK**; the new `TestSeededContainment` suite pins the property across budgets straddling the regime boundary at two coverage targets, plus feature-preservation guards (seeds still included at generous budgets; seeds never consume the whole budget). The first draft of the new test passed vacuously because its centrality-ladder edges used `calls` — itself a cross-cutting type, so every node became a seed candidate; the committed fixture uses `references` and documents why.
- **smart-test's per-PR gate can no longer silently skip the top-level executable surface (INV-lizor / INV-kinin — the trust substrate under every other gate).** Recounted 2026-08-01 before fixing: the gate was structurally blind to vendor hook dirs, `.githooks/**`, `_shared` subdirectories, `scripts/lib/**`, untracked files, and fixture-only edits — and in every blind case it printed *"No Python source files changed - skipping tests"* even when source demonstrably changed, then `ci.yml` skipped pytest on the 0-test manifest. Four closures, all over-selecting rather than under-selecting: **(1) untracked files enter change enumeration** (`git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`, scoped to test/source surfaces so pending tracker `.ops` — untracked by design almost continuously — cannot leak in and fire the fallback every run; previously the sole coverer of a new branch was invisible until committed); **(2) fixture edits select their owning suite** (a fixture changes a gate's *inputs* without running the gate — `tests/fixtures/**` → root suite, `packages/X/tests/fixtures/**` → that package's tests); **(3) unmapped top-level changes union the whole root suite, disclosed** — replacing the dead `CHANGED_TOP_LEVEL_SOURCES` variable that nothing read, beneath a comment describing a check that never existed (its regex was also `.py`-only under `_shared/`, so wiring it up unchanged would have resurrected the pre-WI-bisar `.sh` blindness); **(4) `top_level_test_map.py` claims `.agent/hooks/<vendor>/**`** via name-match UNIONED with an existence-checked cross-vendor parity floor (`test_touch_heartbeat.py`, `test_session_start_respawn.py` — every vendor hook is a thin wrapper over `_shared` logic, so the wiring tests must run for any vendor change). `.githooks/**` stays deliberately unmapped with the decision recorded in the module docstring — the root-suite fallback covers it. The two-sided `KNOWN_UNREACHABLE` ratchet now enumerates vendor dirs as claimed domain; three exemptions retired (`test_session_start_respawn`, `test_session_start_agent_notes`, `test_stop_hook_state_write_discipline`). The truthful skip message ("no test-relevant files changed (docs/config only)") survives only on the branch that can actually know it.
  - **The extended ratchet caught its own author before the PR was even cut:** `tests/test_transcript_permissions.py` (added the same day for INV-todig) was name-unreachable — "perms" is not a prefix of "permissions" — meaning future changes to the permission contract would have selected nothing. Renamed to `tests/test_transcript_perms.py`, matching the contract file it pins. Route 7 of INV-kinin (CLI-driven sole-coverer tests invisible to the import slice) remains open by design here: it needs coverage-map selection, not a name rule, and is recorded as the accepted residual.
- **The transcript pipeline now writes owner-only files — a secret's copy is never more readable than its origin (INV-todig).** `.env` is mode 0600, but every file the transcript pipeline created inherited the process umask (002 here): per-session transcripts, the promoted `.last_*`/`.second_to_last_*` slots, injection history, and the training log all landed at 664, inside a 2775 setgid archive directory — so any command whose output contains a credential (a plain `git remote -v` was the live case, 138 occurrences of two live credentials measured 2026-07-29) was silently republished from owner-only to group-and-world-readable. Scrubbing removed occurrences but could not change the invariant: the next session wrote 664 again, because `mv` and the scrubber both faithfully preserve whatever mode they find. The fix lands at creation, with one home per language layer: `.agent/hooks/_shared/transcript_perms.sh` (umask 077 + idempotent `harden_transcript_file`/`harden_transcript_dir` helpers) is sourced by every shell writer — `sync-transcript.sh`, `rotate-on-session-end.sh`, `launch-transcript-sync.sh`, `archive_scrubbed.sh` — and the two python write sites (`filter-transcript.py` dest, `on_transcript_change.py` injection history + training log) create at 0o600 and re-chmod on every write, so the months of pre-contract 664 files self-heal on next touch rather than waiting for a manual sweep. Rotation additionally hardens the four global slots explicitly (mv preserves the source's mode) and strips the archive root's legacy setgid bit — with an explicit `g-s`, because GNU chmod *preserves* a directory's setgid under numeric modes, so a plain `chmod 700` leaves 2700 with group propagation intact (measured live). Live artifacts remediated in the same change: 241 archive dirs to 0700, all archived/gzipped transcripts to 0600, the 1.2 GB training log to 0600.
  - **Testing.** `tests/test_transcript_permissions.py` (12 tests) drives the real scripts under `umask 002` — the exact failure condition — never a mock: fresh per-session dest born 0600, pre-existing 664 dest healed on next write, rotation leaves no group-readable slot and a 0700 archive tree, a seeded 2775 setgid archive root comes out exactly 0700, `archive_scrubbed` output is 0600 with the fail-safe contract intact (line-count validation still green), injection-history and training-log writers born 0600, plus a parity guard in the `test_touch_heartbeat` style: every shell writer must source `transcript_perms.sh` and call `harden_transcript_umask`, so a writer added without the wiring goes red instead of silently recreating the downgrade.
- **Container-member emission is now a locked axis of the G2 parity matrix — `emits_enum_members` / `emits_abstract_members` (WI-duguk).** Reverse-slice class expansion is spec-🟩 across seven container kinds, but the expansion only does anything where the *analyzer* emitted the container's members as symbols. Measured on a uniform probe corpus generated into `/tmp`: **no analyzer that emits an `enum` emits any of its variants — 0 of 5** (typescript, java, rust, csharp, swift), and abstract-type members are missing for typescript, rust and swift while go, java and csharp emit them. The consumer consequence is not cosmetic: `slice --entry Color --language rust --reverse` returns **1 node** (the container alone) against **2** for a container whose members exist, so a consumer reads "this enum is dead" or "this trait has no users" when neither is true. The new columns measure **span nesting rather than `contains` edges**, because the matrix runs one analyzer in isolation and `contains` is minted downstream by the containment linker — the defect is a **missing node, not a missing edge**. Two columns rather than one, because the families fail independently and a merged column would let a language pass on its abstract members while its enum variants stayed invisible. `docs/hypergumbo-spec.md` keeps the 🟩 on the slicer mechanism (which genuinely expands all seven kinds) and now names the vacuous cells inline, so the delivered-mark no longer reads as an outcome guarantee.
  - **The filed framing was wrong on the scope axis, and the root cause was one level deeper.** WI-duguk scoped to three kinds — trait (1 node), enum (5 nodes), module (0 nodes, "untestable") — from a **self-analysis** substrate, i.e. hypergumbo run on a Python repo. Re-measured: `module` containment works (ruby 100%; **20+ analyzers emit `kind="module"`**), and the zero was an artifact of INV-hojus collapsing Python module-kind into file-kind two weeks before that substrate was taken. The real axis is **(language, container-kind)**, which a per-kind scoping cannot express: scala trait works while rust trait does not; csharp interface works while typescript interface does not. Two live holes the filing never looked at — **typescript `interface` and swift `protocol`** — sit outside its stated scope entirely, and its 42.9%-effective reading of `interface` was corpus dilution rather than a partial mechanism. Neither the slicer nor the containment linker is at fault; the linker has handled trait/enum/struct containers since 2026-02-08 and roots every container whose members exist.
  - **Not by design, established by counter-precedent.** No ADR, spec passage or doc anywhere excludes enum variants or member signatures from being symbols, and the niche analyzers already emit them: verified live, D's `enum Color` yields `field Color.red` / `field Color.green` with dotted names precisely so the containment linker roots them (the WI-sakug / WI-jusus field-emission lineage). The coverage story runs backwards from the usual one — the mainstream analyzers are the gap.
  - **Non-vacuity, in both directions.** The new columns produce **3 hard passes** (csharp/go/java `emits_abstract_members`) alongside **8 strict xfails**, so the check is proven able to yield a positive rather than being green-by-construction. Holes are `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)`, never imperative `pytest.xfail()`, so fixing an analyzer XPASSes its cell and forces the entry's removal in the same PR. **Scope disclosed:** the matrix covers the eight core-availability fixture languages, so the scala, cpp and kotlin enum gaps this investigation also measured are real but *not* gated here — they need grammar wheels the matrix deliberately does not require. Python and JavaScript are marked not-applicable with a written re-eval trigger (Python's enum and Protocol are both classes, already locked by `emits_field`; JavaScript has neither construct), as is Go's enum (its idiom is a `const` block whose members are siblings of the type, not nested in its body).
- **Doc/config-family `node.id` conforms to the ADR-0036 grammar; the limb the self-corpus structurally cannot measure now has a gate (INV-dulah).** `analyze.base.make_doc_symbol_ids` emitted `{lang}:{path}:{kind}:{start_line}:{name}` — kind third, no span, name last — and its own docstring recorded the deferral on the premise that conformance "would re-key every node and is a separate, larger decision." Measurement retired that framing rather than the work: against the canonical right-anchored parse (`span, name, kind = parts[-3:]`) **the kind word landed in the span slot**, so every id from all eleven adopters (rst/scss/vue/svelte/puppet/robot/pony/twig/sparql/kdl/astro) failed `id_format` with `malformed_span_segment` and could not be parsed back into its slots at all. These were not ids in a different-but-workable convention; they were unparseable. The helper now **delegates to `make_symbol_id`** rather than re-implementing the format — an f-string copy of the grammar is precisely how `js_ts.py` and `json_config.py` each opted out of the shared minter's guarantees — which also inherits the WI-sikar always-on name-slot sanitization and thereby repairs a genuine **six-segment** break the old shape shipped: svelte's `on:click` event symbols carried an id name-slot of `button:click`, whose colon shifted every anchor. **Identity-safe:** `stable_id` is computed from the same argument set either way, so it is byte-identical and no `*_scheme` bumps (the scheme-bump principle — a bump follows an *altered* existing computed value). Consumer-visible for `node.id` on these languages; no `SCHEMA_VERSION` change (the field's schema type is unchanged), matching the route-marker sweep's treatment of the same class of change.
  - **The config family folded on too — found by grep, not by the gate.** `ini`, `requirements`, and `properties` each carried a *private copy* of the same broken shape (`requirements`/`properties` at only **four** segments, with no span at all), so they were invisible to any measurement scoped to the shared helper's callers. All three now mint through it. Two `requirements` sites additionally dropped a **role fossil** — the id kind-slot held `url_requirement` / `editable` while `Symbol.kind` is `requirement` (and `make_doc_stable_id` was already being passed `requirement`, so `stable_id` is unchanged); the roles keep their existing homes in `meta["source_type"]` / `meta["editable"]`, the same lossless fold the three manifest analyzers used. Their three private `_make_symbol_id` helpers are deleted, and the three `BRANCHES_test_*` format tests that pinned the old shape are retired with a pointer to the shared helper's own tests — the same disposition the doc-family analyzers' branch tests already carry.
  - **Deliberately NOT folded: `bibtex` and `bitbake`.** Their five sites (`bitbake.py` 192/302/342/382, `bibtex.py` 125 — exactly the set WI-talos Ruling 3 enumerates) set `stable_id = symbol_id`, so the composite id *is* their stable identity and correcting `node.id` would change `stable_id`, falsifying v9's unqualified digest-compatibility claim. That is WI-banod T1, blocked on a **release** rather than on effort.
  - **Testing — and why this limb was invisible for so long.** `test_doc_family_id_parity.py` runs the full pipeline over 14 generated single-language cases and gates two properties per case, each with its own measured strict-xfail hole set: canonical five-segment grammar (14/14 green; was 14/14 red) and id kind-slot equals a registered `Symbol.kind` (12 green, 2 held). Two independent suppressions had to be defeated to see anything at all: **zero of these file types are tracked in this repo**, so a self-analysis reports `id_format: 0` while saying nothing about the family; and even with such files present the default pipeline filters these node kinds, so `include_docs=True` is load-bearing (rst emits 2 sections through the analyzer and **0** through a default run). The corpus is generated into `tmp_path` rather than committed under `tests/fixtures/`, for a reason measured the hard way: committing it added 1 `axis_conformance` error and 3 `id_format` warnings to the full-suite **self-tree ratchet** (ceiling 0 for every unbaselined cell) because the gate deliberately preserves two producer defects as holes. The general form, inverse to the known in-place-fixture hazard: *a gate that deliberately preserves a defect cannot host its corpus inside the tree the project ratchets against itself.*
  - **The two held holes are release-gated, not merely unfixed.** `pony` emits `kind="actor"`, which is not in the symbol-kind catalog (an ADR-0027 vocabulary call, and independently an `axis_conformance` error); `robot` passes `kind="test_case"` while building a `kind="test"` Symbol — a property recorded on this invariant since 2026-06-22. In both cases the `kind` argument feeds `make_doc_stable_id`, so correcting the word churns `stable_id`, putting them behind the same WI-talos v9/v10 sequencing as WI-banod T1. Recorded as `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` markers, never an imperative `pytest.xfail()` — the imperative form can never XPASS, so it would record each violation while silently disabling the ratchet meant to close it.
- **The transcript secret scrubber is now wired into session-end rotation, fail-safe by construction.** Completes the mechanism added in the previous release note, which shipped the scrubber and backfill tool but deliberately left the hooks alone after review found the first wiring attempt destroyed transcripts. Secrets are now redacted at every point the pipeline retains bytes: the archive write, the demote, the promote, the crashed-session orphan sweep, and a **new glob-driven stray sweep** for transcripts whose PID file is gone — a class the PID-keyed sweep could never reach (one live example was 16.8MB from 2026-07-12, unreachable by any code path). **The four hand-rolled `gzip -c src > dest` pipelines collapse into one `archive_scrubbed` helper** (`.agent/hooks/_shared/archive_scrubbed.sh`), because four call sites each independently getting failure handling right is precisely what failed: `gzip -c <readable file>` essentially cannot fail, so the unconditional `rm`/`mv` that followed it was safe for years, and inserting a fallible process in front broke that invariant invisibly. The helper writes a temp, validates it (**non-empty + `gzip -t` + equal uncompressed line count**), and only then moves it into place; the caller removes the source *only* on a validated archive, and rotation **rescues** an un-archivable source to `<archive>/…​.rescued` via a same-filesystem `mv` before the demote can clobber its path. Line-count equality is the check that matters most: a scrubber dying mid-stream yields a gzip-VALID, plausible, silently truncated archive that `gzip -t` cannot detect. Also hardened: invoked as `python3 <script>` rather than relying on the executable bit (a lost `+x` was one of four demonstrated data-loss routes), `--repo-root` passed at every call site (deriving it from `__file__` reads the *script's* repo and silently scrubs nothing when the target is elsewhere), `CDPATH= cd -P --` for path derivation, and the scrubber's stderr **no longer suppressed** — the old `2>/dev/null` discarded the one line reporting that a configured secret was skipped, while the adjacent comment claimed failures were reported loudly.
  - **Testing.** `tests/test_transcript_scrub_wiring.py` is the first test of this wiring at all: the five pre-existing suites that drive rotation create no `.env`, so the scrubber took its passthrough branch and **every one of them passed identically whether it redacted, did nothing, or had a broken key regex**. The new suite asserts both halves — a planted token is gone from the promoted slot *and* the archived `.gz`, and content survives four sabotage modes (missing script, lost `+x`, path is a directory, dies mid-stream) plus a truncation attempt. Both new guards were **mutation-tested**: deleting the line-count check fails 3 tests, and making source removal unconditional again fails 3. `tests/test_watcher_lifecycle.py`'s sandbox now copies the scrubber and helper — omitting them made it a repo where the scrubber is missing, which is how it went red.
- **Transcript secret scrubber + corpus backfill (`scripts/scrub-transcript-corpus`).** Session transcripts capture every command the agent runs, so an ordinary `git remote -v` banks a credential-bearing URL on disk permanently. Measured on this repo: **584 occurrences of four live `.env` credentials across 35 retained files**, in four stores no session hook can reach — 27 gzipped archives, the **1.19 GB `.agent/.training-data.jsonl`** (explicitly maintained for finetuning, so a credential there is on a path into model weights), `.deprecated-datasets/`, and a `.current_session_transcript.<sid>.jsonl` whose PID file is gone. That last one is structurally unreachable by the existing orphan sweep, which iterates PID files and derives the transcript name from them; the backfill globs transcripts instead. `.agent/hooks/_shared/scrub_secrets.py` provides the redaction primitive: secrets come from a gitignored `.secrets_for_scrubbing_upon_archive.txt` (committed `.example` documents the format) plus a conservative `.env` heuristic. Dry-run by default; `--apply` required; gzip members are swapped in only after the replacement validates as a non-empty stream. Scrubbing is idempotent and never prints a secret — diagnostics carry counts and `.env` key names only. **Not wired into the session-end hooks:** an earlier revision was, and adversarial review demonstrated that inserting a fallible process between transcript bytes and their only copy turned a safe unconditional `rm`/`mv` into silent data loss (a 20-byte empty gzip, source deleted, exit 0, no stderr) and turned an existing in-tree test red. Hook wiring is a follow-up that must carry fail-safe archive writes and an end-to-end test.
  - **Design notes, each from a demonstrated defect.** Processing is **binary and chunked with an overlap carry**, never decoded: the first version read text with `errors="replace"` and universal newlines, which silently rewrote non-UTF-8 bytes to U+FFFD, collapsed CRLF, split a record on a lone CR — all with no secret present — and, being line-based, let a secret straddling a `\r` evade redaction *and* rewrote the `\r`, making that credential permanently immune. The `.env` heuristic requires a strong credential key **and** a credential-shaped value, because a bare `*_KEY`/`*_PAT` suffix collides with ordinary config: a plausible `CACHE_KEY=packages/hypergumbo-core` would have replaced **376,928 occurrences across 265 files**, irreversibly. A false positive is worse than a miss here, so the 16-character floor also applies to explicitly-listed entries. Secret ordering is `(-length, lexicographic)` — with length alone, two equal-length overlapping secrets left a different fragment per run depending on set iteration order. `mtime` is preserved at nanosecond precision (rotation reads it to show when a session ended), symlinks are resolved before rewriting, hard links warn that a copy survives, and group ownership is restored so a privacy tool cannot widen who may read a transcript under a setgid directory.
- **Canonical-id parity gate over the three manifest/config analyzers (INV-dulah).** `toml_config` / `json_config` / `xml_config` each mint `Symbol.id` values for manifest constructs, and nothing held them to the ADR-0036 grammar as a group — the only signal was whichever violations the self-corpus happened to surface. `test_config_analyzer_id_parity.py` states the contract as four properties over the producers (five anchored segments with a `{start}-{end}` span slot; kind-slot equals `Symbol.kind`; kind-slot names a registered symbol-kind; the folded role survives in `meta`) across five cases covering pyproject scripts, package.json scripts and `main`, composer.json, and a Maven pom with a coordinate dependency. A non-vacuity guard fails if a case stops emitting symbols, so a producer that goes silent cannot turn the properties green by emptying them.
- **Route-marker identity gate widened to the whole producer fleet: 10 new fixture cases and four measured properties (WI-zugob).** The route-parity corpus held five one-per-language cases and gated route-marker identity with a single aggregate test over `go`/`java`/`javascript` — so eleven of the thirteen route-marker producers were never measured at all, and the three that were got checked on three properties out of four. This adds fixture cases for `django`, `starlette`, `go-mount`, `ruby`, `php`, `elixir`, `swift`, `grpc`, `play-routes`, `js-named`, and `annotation`, and replaces the aggregate with four per-case property tests, each carrying its own strict-xfail hole set so the sweep can drain one producer at a time: **id kind-slot** (equals `Symbol.kind`, names a registered kind — 9 holes), **id name-slot** (equals `sanitize_id_name_segment(Symbol.name)`, ADR-0036 Ruling 1 — 3 holes), **canonical id grammar** (five anchored segments with a `{start}-{end}` span slot — 3 holes), and **origin provenance** (non-empty `origin` of registered pass-ids plus an `origin_run_id` joining a real `AnalysisRun` — 1 hole). A fifth test asserts each analyzer/linker-direct case actually mints a marker, so the property tests cannot pass vacuously if a producer regresses. Every one of the 16 holes was measured by running the full pipeline over the fixtures, not inferred from reading the producers, and three of them are defects no existing gate could see: go's `r.Mount()` site (unmigrated, violates both slots), the Django class-based-view expander emitting a **6-segment id** (it concatenates the colon-bearing name `django:{view}` straight into `make_symbol_id`, a hard ADR-0036 grammar break — WI-javag), and elixir route markers carrying an **empty `Symbol.origin`** (no producer provenance at all; the previous check iterated `origin` and so passed vacuously on an empty list). Fixtures are now copied out to a temp directory before analysis rather than analyzed in place — under `tests/fixtures/` the pipeline's own test-path heuristics suppressed the grpc linker entirely (byte-identical `.proto`: 4 markers at an ordinary path, 0 under the fixture path), which would have made that case a vacuous green.
- **`analyze.base.make_route_symbol` — the shared route-marker minting chokepoint, plus a name-slot gate (WI-zugob / WI-vuzaf Pattern B).** Every route-marker producer hand-rolled its `Symbol` record, and they drifted apart in the four ways the validators check: an id kind-slot left as the literal `route` fossil (`route` is not a registered symbol kind, so `id_format` fails), an absent `origin_run_id` (`cross_field`), an unregistered `origin` pass-id (`axis_conformance`), and — newly measured here — an id **name-slot** that does not round-trip against `Symbol.name` (ADR-0036 Ruling 1). The new factory encapsulates the shape `framework_patterns.materialize_route_symbols` already proved for the go/js/java trio, so a producer is fixed by routing through it rather than by re-deriving the invariant. **Naming is forced, not stylistic:** `Symbol.name` is `"{METHOD} {path}"` with the handler in `meta.handler_ref` (which is what `linkers/route_handler` already resolves against), because a multi-method registration — Starlette `methods=['GET','POST']`, a Django class-based view expanded per verb — emits several markers at the *same span*, and only the method+path pair keeps their ids distinct; a handler-derived name-slot would collide them. Landed **with its gate and before the producer sweep**, so each migration provably shrinks a measured number: `test_route_marker_id_name_slot_round_trips` asserts the round-trip per language, with not-yet-migrated producers recorded in `NAME_SLOT_HOLES` as a **strict** xfail so a migration XPASSes and forces promotion (the same ratchet idiom as `ROUTE_HOLES`). The measured baseline: java and javascript are already clean; python and rust surface routes as a `concept=route` tag rather than a standalone marker and need their own fixtures to be gated; **`go` is a live violation** — it mints markers inline with `name=handler_name` against an id name-slot built from `"{METHOD} {path}"`, i.e. the same inverted Pattern B that WI-vuzaf filed as Starlette-specific, inside the cohort WI-tufil had declared clean. The remaining producer migrations (go, py.py Django/Starlette/Flask-RESTful, ruby, php, elixir, swift, play_routes/scala, js_ts, grpc, annotation_convention) stay tracked on WI-zugob.
- **Generated per-pathway confidence table closes ADR-0039 R5 Stage B (WI-limom).** Spec §12 explained *how* `Edge.confidence` is derived but never published the values, so the only way to learn a pathway's reliability was to read `evidence_types.py`. `docs/concept-axes.md` — which already enumerated all 125 `Edge.evidence_type` values and already had a `--check` freshness gate — now also carries the `base_confidence` projection: a value→pathways table (80 of 125 seeded, band 0.40–0.95), the two `is_resolved`-conditioned call pathways with both bases, an explicitly disclosed unseeded list (45, where `derive_confidence` returns `None` and the producer keeps its literal), and the derived value inline on each evidence-type bullet for point lookup. Emitted from the registry rather than hand-transcribed — the item deferred precisely to avoid a drift-prone hand-written table — and homed in the existing generated doc rather than a new one, so it inherits the existing pre-commit + `verify-generated` gate instead of adding a second. Spec §12 now owns the *model* and links the table for the *values*, so the two cannot drift.

### Removed
- **`Edge.quality` field removed (WI-riguh / WI-humok; ADR-0039 ruling 4) — SCHEMA_VERSION 0.19.0 → 0.20.0 (breaking output-format change).** The `{score, reason}` block carried zero independent signal: `quality.score` was `round(clamp(confidence), 3)` (verified on 110,533/110,533 corpus edges) and `quality.reason` encoded the emitter mechanism, not a confidence tier. The one-version deprecation window (opened at 0.14.6) has elapsed. Consumers read `confidence` + `confidence_source` + `is_resolved` (and `rank_score` for ranking prominence) instead. `Edge.from_dict` still tolerates a legacy `quality` key on pre-0.20.0 cached artifacts (ignored, not resurrected). The unrelated **`Symbol.quality`** (Node-level, INV-nuzal — declared-but-empty, awaiting a future producer) is unaffected.

### Changed
- **24 more untyped signatures annotated across nine files — INV-zogud 797 → 766, and four more unguarded `Node.text` dereferences found.** Span helpers in the two route linkers, tree-cursor walkers in `fingerprint`, `**kwargs: Any` in the analyzer registry, `RegisteredLinker` in `pass_metadata`, and the two view-template linkers. The view-template pair are the interesting ones: their tree-sitter imports live *inside a `try`*, because these linkers must degrade gracefully when the optional grammar is absent — so the annotations name `Optional["tree_sitter.Parser"]` under `TYPE_CHECKING` and the runtime import graph is untouched. A module-scope import to satisfy an annotation would convert "this linker quietly does nothing" into "the tool fails to start".
  - **`tree_sitter.Node.text` is `bytes | None`, and the correct handling differs per site.** Annotating `view_template_spring`'s node parameters exposed four bare `.text.decode(...)` calls. Two decode text that is immediately *compared* (`== method_short_name`, `!= "ModelAndView"`), where an empty-string default is right because the comparison then fails and existing logic handles the non-match. Two decode text that *becomes a view name emitted as an edge*, where an empty-string default would mint a reference to a view whose name could not be read — a silent wrong-value bug, worse than the crash it replaces. Those two bail instead. A blanket `if X.text else ""` would have satisfied the checker, passed every test, and introduced empty-named edges.
  - **This is now a measured class:** 34 `.text.decode(` sites in `packages/*/src/`, 27 guarded, ~7 not — and the unguarded ones are invisible to mypy until their receiver is annotated, since an untyped parameter makes `node.text` an `Any`. Two files found so far, each by annotating something else, and each *half-guarding its own idiom*. They will keep surfacing one batch at a time, which the blocking whole-tree ratchet turns into a forcing function rather than a leak.
  - `pass_metadata` takes `RegisteredLinker` as a **direct runtime import**, not a type-only one: the module already does `from .linkers.registry import get_all_linkers`, so the import cycle a type-only import would have been avoiding does not exist.

- **17 untyped signatures annotated in `toml_config` / `css` / `latex` — INV-zogud 821 → 797, and two real crash paths surfaced.** `tree_sitter.Node.text` is `bytes | None`, which is invisible while the `node` parameter is untyped. Annotating it showed `toml_config.py` **half-guarding its own idiom**: lines 104 and 111 write `node.text.decode(...) if node.text else ""` while 93 and 95 dereference bare — same file, same pattern, and only the unguarded half can raise `AttributeError` on a text-less key node. Both now follow the file's own majority convention. That is a behaviour change (crash → empty string), chosen because two sibling lines already made exactly that call; the smaller claim is matching them rather than inventing a policy.
  - **The guard rule matters more than the annotations.** `_get_string_value` carries an `if node is None:  # pragma: no cover - defensive` guard, so it is annotated `"tree_sitter.Node | None"` while its five siblings take plain `Node`. Annotating it non-Optional would turn that live guard into an `[unreachable]` error — growing a different bucket and breaking the shrink-only gate, or forcing the deletion of a real guard. A `no cover` pragma means *untested*, not *unreachable*; the span-ternary revert earlier this cycle is what that distinction costs when it is got wrong.
  - `tree_sitter` enters `css.py` and `toml_config.py` as a **`TYPE_CHECKING`-only** import. Not stylistic: tree-sitter is an optional dependency for these analyzers, and a module-scope import converts a graceful "grammar unavailable, skip" into an ImportError at startup.
  - Also fixed a pre-existing narrowing in `scala.py`: the block narrows `_sym is not None`, then discards the narrowed local and re-reads `lookup_result.symbol.id`, which mypy cannot narrow. Same object at runtime; it now uses `_sym.id`.

- **11 stale `# type: ignore` directives removed — INV-zogud 832 → 821.** Each was written because someone hit a real error at that line, and every one has since been fixed by something else: `taint.py`'s `return-value`, `serve.py`'s `type-arg`, `cli.py`'s `name-defined` forward-ref, and three `no-redef` suppressions on the Python 3.10 `tomli` fallback imports. The directives outlived the problems. Two were **bare** `# type: ignore` (`r_lang.py`, `starlark.py`), which suppress *every* error on their line rather than a named one — the more valuable removals, since an unused bare ignore is a standing invitation for an unrelated error to appear there and be swallowed silently.
  - **Self-verifying by construction:** a `# type: ignore` is inert at runtime, so the only way removing one can matter is if it was suppressing a live error — in which case mypy reports it immediately and the now-blocking ratchet refuses the change. Delta was exactly −11, one per directive, so none were load-bearing.
  - **The `tomli` trio looked version-dependent and is not.** `tomllib` is stdlib on 3.11+ with `tomli` as the 3.10 fallback, so an ignore unused on one interpreter could in principle be required on another. Checked: mypy runs only in `ci.yml`, only on Python 3.11 — the nightly 3.10–3.13 matrix runs tests, not the type checker — so a version-dependent directive cannot affect the type gate whichever branch an interpreter takes.
  - Same decay pattern as the stale prose corrected earlier this cycle (the false `availability="core"` claim, three dead `service` container references), in machine-readable form. The difference is that mypy can *detect* this kind, which is why it is the kind that gets cleaned up; nothing detects a comment that has quietly stopped being true.

- **Route-marker `Symbol.name` is now `"{METHOD} {path}"` across every producer (WI-zugob / WI-vuzaf Pattern B / WI-javag) — the one consumer-visible change in this convergence.** Route markers are the synthesized `meta.framework_role in {route, route_mount, route_include}` symbols that represent a registered endpoint (as opposed to the handler function it dispatches to). Producers had drifted into five different naming conventions — the handler's own name (go's mount, js/ts), a framework-prefixed view name (`django:list_users`, `starlette:list_users`), a bare view name (Flask-RESTful), a dotted expansion (`django:AdminView.get`), and a bespoke include form (`-> /admin admin.Routes`) — while their *ids* mostly carried the method+path pair, so a consumer reconstructing a node's name from its documented id got a different string (ADR-0036 Ruling 1). All of them now mint through `analyze.base.make_route_symbol` and derive both name and id name-slot from the same method+path pair. **Nothing is lost:** handler identity lives in `meta["handler_ref"]` / `meta["handler_name"]`, view identity in `meta["view_name"]`, the included module in `meta["module_ref"]` — the keys `linkers/route_handler` already resolved against. It never read `Symbol.name`, so route→handler edge recovery is unchanged; empirically, exactly **two assertions in the whole test tree** read a route marker's name, and both were using it as a proxy for the handler. The direction is forced rather than stylistic: a multi-method registration (Starlette `methods=["GET","POST"]`, a Django class-based view expanded per verb) emits several markers at one span, so only the method+path pair keeps their ids distinct. Affected: py.py (Django, Starlette, Flask-RESTful), the Django class-based-view expander, `go.py`'s `r.Mount()`, `js_ts.py` (both route paths), and `play_routes.py`'s module include (`-> /admin admin.Routes` → `-> /admin`).

### Changed
- **`mutable-override` eliminated 105 → 0 by changing TWO declaration lines — INV-zogud 957 → 832 (WI-rokup).** Every one of the 105 errors carried a byte-identical message naming the same base class: 105 analyzer subclasses across all three language packages each narrowed `TreeSitterAnalyzer.grammar_module` or `.language_pack_name` from `str | None` to `str`, which mypy reports as a **covariant override of a MUTABLE attribute**. That was **11% of the entire strict surface from two declarations** in `analyze/base.py`. The error is reported at each *subclass* while the defect lives in the *base*, so reading any individual one tells you nothing — it only resolves when you notice all 105 messages are identical. Both attributes are now read-only `@property`s returning `str | None`: **zero subclass edits**, and an attribute that is only ever read stops claiming to be mutable.
  - **Runtime-neutral by MRO, verified on real analyzers rather than argued.** A subclass class-body assignment precedes the base in the MRO, so `self.grammar_module` still returns the string; a subclass declaring neither falls through to the property and gets `None`. Checked live: `CppAnalyzer` → `tree_sitter_cpp`, `IniTreeSitterAnalyzer` → language-pack `ini`, base → `None` for both. 16,446 tests pass — the change touches the base class of every tree-sitter analyzer in the tool, and `_check_grammar_available` / `_create_parser` both branch on these values, so a silent grammar-disabling regression was the risk being tested for.
  - **The tempting fix would have broken 60 analyzers.** `ClassVar[str] = ""` is tidier and matches the `lang` / `pass_id` sentinels a few lines above — and it is a live behaviour change: five `is not None` checks become permanently true, so every language-pack analyzer would call `importlib.import_module("")`, and `_get_config_dict` feeds `compute_config_fingerprint`, a **persisted cache key**, so every analyzer's fingerprint would change. `ClassVar` alone does not silence the error and `Final` makes it worse (adding "cannot assign to final name" at all 105 sites). The two residual sharp edges of the property form were grepped before committing: a class-level read now yields the property object rather than `None`, and `self.grammar_module = ...` would raise — every read in `packages/*/src/` is `self.`-qualified and there are zero such assignments in the repo.
- **INV-zogud drain: `ir.py` / `analyze/base.py` typed, 20 further `type-arg` errors.** Types read off the construction site, not the name: `global_symbols` is `dict[str, Symbol]` because `register_symbol` does `global_symbols[symbol.name] = symbol`; the occurrence-counter key tuple leads with `str | None` because `protocol_origin` is declared `Optional[str]`. Both are core modules and both revealed **zero** hidden errors, which refutes the centrality hypothesis the previous batches suggested — the predictor of cascade risk is interface polymorphism, not how central the module is.
- **INV-zogud drain: `taint.py` / `sketch.py` / `io_boundary.py` typed, 1036 → 992 — and every hidden error the bare generics were masking was real.** Removing 43 `type-arg` errors revealed **37** others across four codes, taking four measure-fix rounds to converge. None were noise: `list` is **invariant**, so a `TaintSource | TaintSink` union alias needed `Sequence` to accept a concrete `list[TaintSource]` at its call sites; one function bound `edge` to a `DdgEdge` in two loops and to a call-edge **dict** in three more, which was invisible while both were `Any`; `_merge_with_user_override` turned out to be **type-preserving** (hand it sources, get sources back), so a union return would have let a merged source+sink mapping be assigned onto a sources-only catalog field — it now carries a `TypeVar`; and one dict genuinely holds two nested summaries plus an int count.
  - **The pattern behind all of it.** A bare `dict`/`list` is not laziness, it is **load-bearing ambiguity** — the places hardest to annotate are exactly the places where the code is polymorphic and the bare generic was letting nobody decide. Three of the reveals were incorrect annotations of ours, each caught by the whole-tree ratchet before reaching a PR. Consequence for the remaining ~360 `type-arg` errors: that count is a **lower bound on the work, not an upper one**, and the drain is not monotonic.
  - Types are the measured ones: `list[Edge]` where the body does attribute access (`edge.meta`, `edge.dst`) versus `dict[str, dict[str, Any]]` where it subscripts (`node.get("meta")`) — both appear in one signature in `io_boundary._compute_external_potential`, which is precisely what a blanket rule would get wrong. Cross-module references (`DdgEdge` from `cfg`, `Edge` from `ir`) are `TYPE_CHECKING` imports: no cycle exists either way, but the runtime import graph is unchanged for modules the CLI loads on every invocation.
- **INV-zogud drain: `compact.py` typed, 1080 → 1036 — and filling in bare generics turned out to REVEAL errors, not just annotate.** A bare `dict` is implicitly `dict[Any, Any]`, which silences every downstream type error inside it. Parameterising 44 `type-arg` sites in one file therefore dropped the total by 44 **and simultaneously grew three other codes** (`arg-type`, `index`, `return-value`) as genuine mismatches came out from behind the `Any`. So the 445-error `type-arg` class is not a batch of annotation chores — it is 445 lifted rocks, the drain is **not monotonic**, and each batch needs a whole-tree ratchet run rather than a per-file one (the per-file run reported this file clean while the whole-tree run found all three, exactly the measurement error ADR-0014's whole-tree correction exists to prevent).
  - **Two of the three revealed errors were real; one was mine.** `compute_tier_distribution` is keyed by `supply_chain_tier` — an int, as its own `-> Dict[int, int]` return type says — and a blanket `Counter` → `Counter[str]` substitution across five sites got the fifth wrong. The blocking ratchet shipped hours earlier caught it before it reached a PR, which is the mechanism working on its first real use. The genuine finding: `_annotate_node_centrality` looked up `centrality.get(n.get("id"), 0.0)`, where `n.get("id")` is `Any | None` and a `None` key can never be present — so an id-less projected node silently scored `0.0`, indistinguishable from a node genuinely at 0.0.
  - Annotations are the measured types, not blanket `Any`: adjacency maps are `Dict[str, set[str]]` of node ids, `UnionFind` elements `list[str]`, frequency counters `Counter[str]` (and `Counter[int]` where the key is a tier). `dict[str, Any]` appears only where the value really is heterogeneous projected JSON — a blanket `[Any, Any]` fill would satisfy the checker while laundering unknown types as declared ones, which is worse than the missing annotation.
- **The whole-tree mypy strict ratchet now BLOCKS merges (WI-rabum), and its blocker turned out to be a defect in the ratchet rather than a reason to wait.** The documented ramp — "grind 1080 → 0, *then* flip CI to blocking" — reads as a hard dependency and is not one: `check-mypy-ratchet` fails only when an error code **grows** against the baseline, never on the absolute count, so 1080 errors and 0 errors gate identically. `--mode=strict` exits 0 on dev today. The cost of the old rung is on the record: WI-basat existed because seven errors accumulated silently over ten days, not because anyone ignored a failure but because **a non-blocking warning is by definition a signal nobody must act on**.
  - **The one risk the item had to weigh is now closed rather than accepted.** WI-rabum's open question was a mypy release recategorising errors and blocking every PR at once, independent of any code change. Investigating it found something worse and older: the baseline recorded `total` and `by_code` but **not the mypy version that produced them**, while CI installed `mypy~=2.1` — which admits 2.2. A shrink-only baseline whose measuring instrument can change under it was never a ratchet; in warning mode a release could have moved every number and nobody could have told tooling drift from code drift. So the baseline now records `mypy_version`, the ratchet **refuses to compare counts across versions** (a distinct `INSTRUMENT MISMATCH` diagnostic instead of reporting six unrelated codes as "grown" — the most confusing possible way to fail), and the pin is tightened to `~=2.1.0` so a bump is a deliberate edit that must carry its own `--update-baseline`. The mismatch blocks **even when counts shrank**: a shrink measured by a different instrument is not evidence of progress, and banking it would ratify tooling drift as a drain.
  - **The risk was not hypothetical — it was already live, and this PR's own CI proved it.** The dev extras in `hypergumbo-core` and `hypergumbo-tracker` declared `mypy~=2.1`, which admits 2.2 and 2.3; a fresh CI install resolved **mypy 2.3.0** while the baseline had been measured locally at 2.1.0. So the instrument already differed between a developer's machine and a clean install, silently, with the ratchet comparing counts across the two and reporting the difference as code drift. The dev extras are now pinned to `~=2.1.0` alongside the CI job, so one series is used everywhere; **adopting a newer mypy is now a deliberate change that must carry its own re-measured baseline**, which is exactly what the mismatch check exists to force.
  - **The flip is four edits, three of them individually silent — so all four are pinned by a static guard.** Removing `continue-on-error` makes the job red and changes nothing on its own, because the merge verdict is `ci-complete` and a job absent from its `needs` cannot fail it; being in `needs` is itself insufficient, since `ci-complete` runs `if: always()` and decides by explicitly testing each result. That is the same shape the old rung had — a job that looked like a gate and gated nothing. `tests/test_check_mypy_ratchet_blocking.py` pins no-`continue-on-error`, membership in `ci-complete.needs`, that the verdict actually reads `needs.mypy.result`, `--mode=strict`, the patch-series pin, and that the committed baseline records its instrument. Two revert paths were mutation-tested. Disclosed gap: `ci.yml` is not a mapped source, so editing *it* selects no tests (the INV-kinin class) — a silent revert is caught by the twice-daily full suite, not per-PR.

### Fixed
- **PHP emitted only classes — `interface`, `trait` and `enum` were all invisible (INV-tihim).** Three of PHP's four type containers had no branch in the symbol walk, so an `interface` declared in the same file surfaced as an **`external_symbol`** (the boundary synthesizer's placeholder for a name with no in-tree definition), and members declared inside an interface or trait were emitted **bare** — `area` rather than `Shape.area` — because the enclosing-owner walk recognised `class_declaration` alone while the sibling `_enclosing_type_name` helper already walked all four. Fixed with **one branch keyed off a container→kind map** rather than three copies of the class branch, so a fifth container cannot be added to `_PHP_TYPE_CONTAINERS` and silently skipped. **All three of PHP's stacked defects fall out of that one change:** the interface becomes a real node, its member is owner-qualified, and the relationship self-corrects from `extends`→`external_symbol` to **`implements`→`interface`** — because the inheritance linker classifies the edge by the *target's* kind, which is why Java gets this right despite lumping extends and implements into the same `base_classes` meta. PHP now emits `dispatches_to: Shape.area -> Square.area` and its parity cell is promoted from hole to hard pass.
- **TypeScript records `abstract` on abstract classes (audit-findings 0018).** Five languages (java, csharp, php, scala, kotlin) mark an abstract class `kind="class"` + `modifiers=["abstract"]`, which is where `is_abstract_type` reads it; TypeScript emitted an empty modifier list, so its abstract classes were indistinguishable from concrete ones. Nothing had to be inferred — the grammar hands the analyzer a distinct `abstract_class_declaration` node and the Symbol simply never set the field.
- **C++ emits pure virtual methods, and a class declaring one is marked abstract (audit-findings 0018).** The member walk filtered out every function declaration, so `virtual int area() = 0;` vanished entirely and an abstract base had no method to dispatch *from*; `modifiers` was likewise empty on every C++ symbol. Both closed. **Deliberately scoped to pure virtuals (`= 0`)**: a non-pure declaration (`virtual int perim();`) has its definition in another translation unit and hypergumbo has no decl/def merging for C++, so emitting those would produce two symbols for one method across a header/source split — a pure virtual has no definition anywhere by construction and cannot duplicate. Guarded by a test asserting non-pure declarations are still skipped.
  - **C++'s parity cell stays red, on a different cause, and the reason is worth recording.** Every ingredient is now present — `Shape::area` exists, containment roots it, the class carries `abstract` — and no `dispatches_to` appears, because `type_hierarchy._extends_admits_dispatch` asks whether the **child** is abstract while a C++ abstract-base hierarchy has the abstract **parent** (`extends: Square -> Shape`, with `Square` concrete). The rule is correct for the case it was written for (Go interface embedding and C# interface inheritance, where both endpoints are abstract so the two tests coincide) and wrong for abstract-base inheritance. Widening it changes dead-code reachability across all four `NO_VIRTUAL_EXTENDS_LANGUAGES`, so it is filed rather than bundled here.

### Fixed
- **Rust gets precise interface dispatch, and the member-name separator vocabulary gets one home (INV-tihim).** `linkers/type_hierarchy` — the language-neutral linker that emits interface-method → impl-method `dispatches_to` for every language — split qualified member names on `#` and `.` but **not** `::`, so `_get_method_short_name("MyTrait::method")` returned the whole string and `_get_class_name_from_method` returned `None`. It was structurally incapable of firing for Rust, which is the reason a Rust-specific dispatch linker exists at all. Rust now emits `dispatches_to: Shape::area -> Square::area`, matching every other language, and its parity-column cell is promoted from a strict-xfail hole to a hard pass **in the same PR** — which is what the strict marker forces.
  - **The fact now has one home, and it was recorded in six places.** New `hypergumbo_core.member_names` owns `MEMBER_NAME_SEPARATORS = ("#", "::", ".")` plus `split_member_name` / `member_owner` / `member_short_name`, and six language-agnostic sites were swept onto it: `linkers/containment`, `linkers/method_call_recovery`, `linkers/type_hierarchy`, `receiver_blind_magnets` (whose private copy was ordered `("::", "#", ".")` — wrong, since Ruby's `Foo::Bar#baz` splits at the `#`, so it would have returned `Foo` as the owner), and three sites in `linkers/route_handler` that knew only `("#", ".")` and so could not index a `Type::handler` route. A live-tree linter with a planted-offender test prevents a seventh copy.
  - **Deliberately NOT folded into `qualified_name_axis`.** That module maps a language to its *module-path* separator (`php` → `\`), while this one is the owner↔member separator in `Symbol.name`. They coincide for Rust and diverge for PHP, whose members are emitted dotted — folding them would silently mis-split every PHP member. The linter therefore exempts any literal containing a separator outside this vocabulary (a `\`-bearing set is asking the other module's question), and exempts per-language analyzer packages, where knowing your own language's separators is correct rather than a re-declaration.
  - **`linkers/rust_trait_dispatch` narrowed rather than retired, and no edge is lost.** It fanned `dispatches_to` from a trait to *every* method of implementing structs — deliberate over-approximation, since a Rust trait impl looks unreachable to a naive call graph and the alternative was declaring ~3,000 live functions dead. Now that the shared linker covers the trait-required methods precisely, this one skips them and keeps only the inherent methods the shared one cannot reach. **An empty requirement set means "cover everything", not "cover nothing"** — an external trait, or an analyzer that failed to emit members, falls back to the old behaviour. Measured on three fixtures: trait method → precise `method:Shape::area -> Square::area`; inherent method → `trait:Shape -> Square::helper` retained; marker trait with zero requirements → `trait:Marker -> Square::helper` retained.
  - **The ADR-0023 §3 allow-list entry added under INV-fimon stays, and is now permanently legitimate.** The `(trait, rust) → (method, rust)` partition still carries both `contains` and `dispatches_to`, because inherent-method dispatch keeps the trait anchor. The ADR's own caveat — that the entry's removal was conditional, not guaranteed — is the condition failing, as written.

### Added
- **`constructed_from` swept to six more languages, with a parity column and the first framework YAMLs that key on it (WI-nopod).** The Python pilot proved the fact could be recorded; this makes it usable. **`fastapi.yaml` and `flask.yaml` now tag `app = FastAPI()` / `Flask(__name__)` as `concept: application` and `APIRouter()` / `Blueprint()` as `concept: router`** — the first framework integrations written against a *construction* site. The contrast is the point: `aiohttp.yaml` reaches its `Application` with a plain `base_class` rule because you subclass it; FastAPI's is constructed, and until now no rule could reach it at all.
  - **A parity column first, and it changed the plan.** "Eight analyzers need the key" was the scope until `test_constructed_from_parity` measured it. There are **two** hole shapes, not one: six languages were **KEY MISSING** (the analyzer emits the binding and just doesn't stamp the callee — javascript, typescript, go, swift, rust, clojure, all now closed), and three are **NO SYMBOL** (ruby, php, elixir emit no module-level variable at all, so there is nothing to stamp). The second is a prerequisite gap of a different size, filed separately rather than bundled. Holes are strict xfails carrying which shape they are.
  - **One shared helper, not six copies.** `analyze.base.constructed_from_callee` owns the rule and its three narrowings for every tree-sitter analyzer; Clojure's s-expression form is the one genuine exception. The helper falls back to a leading name-shaped child when a grammar names no callee field — which is how **Swift** works without a special case, since its `call_expression` is simply `(simple_identifier, call_suffix)` with no fields at all. Go needed its own unwrap: the `value` field resolves to an `expression_list` rather than being absent, and assuming absence is why Go silently recorded nothing on the first attempt.
  - **The expected callee is per-language, and a single expectation was silently wrong.** Rust's construction idiom is an associated function (`C::new`), Go's is a constructor function (`NewC`), JS uses `new C()`. The column carries the expectation per language after a shared one quietly mis-passed Rust.
  - **Caught by measuring end-to-end, not by review:** the first version of the YAML patterns was appended to the end of `fastapi.yaml`, which put them inside the `linkers:` list rather than `patterns:`. yamllint passed, the file parsed, and `load_framework_patterns` returned **zero** of them. A regression test now asserts the loader actually sees them, because nothing else would.
- **`Symbol.meta["constructed_from"]` — the framework-object binding, which nothing recorded (WI-nopod).** A whole class of frameworks is configured by *constructing* an object rather than decorating or subclassing one (`app = FastAPI()`, `Base = declarative_base()`, `runner = CliRunner()`), and a YAML author had no key to write a pattern against. Python now stamps the callee on the bound variable, qualification intact (`fastapi.FastAPI`, not `FastAPI`) so a namespaced callee stays distinguishable from a same-named local. Registered on the `symbol_meta` axis; **`docs/schema.json` regenerated**.
  - **The item's diagnosis was wrong in a way that changed the fix's module.** It reported that the matcher "lacks call/instantiates/constructor_call entry families." The matcher has had a call surface since 2026-01-30 — `UsagePatternSpec` matches `UsageContext` on `(kind, name, position)` — four months before the filing. But ADR-3ccc's model is *"a first-party symbol appears **inside** a framework call"*, and every `position` it defines is an input slot (`args[1]`, `args[0].handler`, a map key, `default`, `block`); WI-nopod's cases invert that, since the symbol to tag is the call's **result**. The item's own alternative — "the substrate already has ~7,939 `instantiates` edges" — fails for a related reason: measured, those are anchored at the **file** (*"this file instantiated FastAPI somewhere"*) and the `app` variable carried no link to `FastAPI` at all. Both proposed fixes matched nothing because the binding was never recorded.
  - **Chosen home is `Symbol.meta`, mirroring `meta.base_classes`** — both record what type a symbol relates to, as metadata a linker can later promote to edges. The decisive property: **the matcher needs no change**, since the existing `meta_match` field takes a `{key: regex}` map over `Symbol.meta`. That is asserted by test, not inferred. The alternatives each cost an ADR ruling — a result-position `UsageContext` widens `symbol_ref` from "the symbol being used" to "the symbol produced by this call" and adds a fifth pattern category to an ADR whose status is already *Implemented*; a new edge type is ADR-0023's territory.
  - **Deliberately narrow in three ways, each guarded by a test.** Only a call result is stamped, so the key stays useful as a filter (`TIMEOUT = 30` carries nothing). Factory and constructor calls are treated identically, because static analysis cannot distinguish them and asserting "this is a class" would be a guess. A **computed callee** (`factories[key]()`, `registry[0].Build()`) records nothing rather than a partial name — a YAML matching on a fiction would fail silently, so absence is the honest signal. Python only: nine analyzers emit `UsageContext`, each with its own hardcoded call-name allowlist, and sweeping all nine before one is proven is the failure mode this codebase has the most scar tissue about.
  - **Found while testing: `meta_match` applies `regex.match`, not `search`.** The natural-looking `(^|\.)FastAPI$` silently fails on `fastapi.FastAPI`; `(.*\.)?FastAPI$` is the form that works. Pinned in a test with an explicit tripwire, so the next author keying on a qualified callee does not rediscover it the hard way.
- **G2 parity column for interface dispatch — and it found five holes nobody was looking for (INV-tihim).** `test_interface_dispatch_parity.py` injects the same fixture into 16 languages (one abstract type declaring one required method, one concrete implementor) and asserts on the **emitted artifact** after the full pipeline: does a `dispatches_to` edge run from the abstract type's method to the implementor's method? Uniform fixtures mean an empty cell is an analyzer or linker gap, never construct-absence. Measured: **8 hard passes** (java, csharp, go, typescript, scala, swift, groovy, python) and **8 strict-xfail holes**, each carrying its measured cause and an owning tracker item. One pipeline run over a polyglot corpus rather than 16 runs — 0.7s instead of ~30s, with per-language xfail granularity preserved.
  - **The item that produced this column named one of the eight causes.** INV-tihim was filed as "one fact, two homes" — the `::` separator divergence — which is real and is *Rust's* cause alone. Probing four languages found three unrelated causes; probing sixteen found **eight**, of which three are producer gaps (php, cpp, elixir), two are consumer-vocabulary gaps (solidity, ruby), one is an anchoring choice (rust), one is a missing edge (dart), and one is an unrelated analyzer crash (kotlin — this fixture has two bodied type declarations, which is exactly the shape that makes that analyzer emit zero type symbols). Generalizing from the cause in hand is the failure this column exists to make impossible.
  - **Two of the holes are the same disease as audit-findings 0018, one layer over — on `Edge.edge_type`.** `edge_types.py` exports a registry-backed `INHERITANCE_EDGE_TYPES = {extends, implements, inherits}`; only `slice.py` and `ranking.py` consume it. `cli.py` hand-rolls a complete copy, `_transitive_bases.py` omits `inherits`, `inherited_calls.py` has `includes` but omits `inherits`, and `type_hierarchy.py` branches inline on two string literals and never consults the registry at all — **five vocabularies, all disagreeing**, against an ADR-0023 §3 instruction to call `edge_types_on_axis(...)` instead. Solidity emits `inherits` and Ruby emits `includes`; both are invisible, so neither gets dispatch despite emitting every symbol correctly. Filed as INV-nosoz rather than folded, since ruby's value is absent from the grouping while solidity's is present-but-ignored — fixing either alone leaves the other broken.
  - **Built to resist the ways this kind of gate goes quiet.** End-to-end rather than analyzer-only, because WI-duguk's column read green for Swift while the live slice was unmoved. Corpus generated into `tmp_path_factory`, because the pipeline suppresses linkers under `tests/fixtures/` paths and because a gate that preserves defects cannot host its corpus inside the tree the project ratchets against itself. Holes are `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` markers, never imperative `pytest.xfail()` — mutation-tested: marking a passing language as a hole XPASSes and fails the run, forcing the entry's removal. Plus a liveness floor, a non-vacuity floor (≥6 hard passes, so the column cannot go green by becoming all-xfail), a scope-disjointness check, and a declared not-applicable map with reasons.
- **`Symbol.kind` gains a type-family taxonomy, and Swift protocols get interface dispatch for the first time (audit-findings 0018 / INV-tihim).** The audit established the vocabulary was sound and the defect was a missing *predicate*: 138 values on one flat axis, so `symbol_kinds_on_axis()` could not express "the abstract types" and every consumer wrote a literal instead. `SymbolKindSpec` now carries `type_family` (`abstract_type` for `interface`/`trait`/`protocol`; `concrete_type` for `class`/`struct`/`enum`/`record`/`object`/`union`/`contract`), with `type_like_kind_names()`, `abstract_type_kind_names()` and `is_abstract_type(kind, modifiers)` as the `Symbol.kind` analogue of `edge_types_on_axis()`. **Deliberately not a new axis under ADR-0024** — no IR field changes and no value is reclassified; it is a taxonomy over the already-declared `language_construct` axis, so nothing touches `stable_id` or the v9/v10 scheme sequencing.
  - **Measured behaviour change.** `linkers/type_hierarchy` gated virtual dispatch on `child_kind == "interface"` — one of the three inherently-abstract kinds — so a Swift `protocol` produced `implements` and **no `dispatches_to` at all**, while the byte-equivalent Java `interface` produced both. That gate is now `is_abstract_type(...)`, which also reaches `abstract class` through the modifier, something the literal could never express. End-to-end on a one-interface/one-implementor fixture, Swift goes from **no dispatch edge** to `dispatches_to: Shape.area -> Square.area`, matching Java exactly. Four sites swept onto the resolver: `type_hierarchy.py`, `inheritance.py` (which held a *correct* abstract set at line 91 and an incorrect one at 426), `_transitive_bases.py` (a mutable-default literal replaced by a `None` sentinel resolved from the registry), and `cli.py`'s class-meta index.
  - **A linter so the copy-a-sibling's-set mechanism cannot recur**, with two principled exemptions rather than an allow-list. `find_partial_abstract_family_literals` flags any language-agnostic literal that enumerates *part* of the abstract family, but exempts (a) expressions guarded by a sibling `language` comparison (`s.language == "graphql" and s.kind in (...)` is a per-language predicate that merely lives in core) and (b) modules whose declared `@register_linker(depends_on=...)` languages cannot produce the missing kind. That second rule is what separates a defect from a correct set: `jackson_dispatch` and `kafka_streams_dispatch` declare `depends_on=[["java"]]` and omit `trait`/`protocol` **correctly** — no Java symbol is ever either — while `type_hierarchy` declares scala/rust/swift among twelve languages and omitting `protocol` is the measured bug. Of eleven literals the naive rule flagged, exactly **five** survive as real defects. The kind→language map backing it carries a written re-evaluation trigger (R10): a new analyzer emitting one of these kinds for an unlisted language silences the linter for it.
- **Audit-findings 0018 — `Symbol.kind` type family: the leak hypothesis is REJECTED and the real defect is a missing consumer predicate (INV-tihim).** Seven language-agnostic consumers hand-roll mutually inconsistent sets of "type-like" kinds, and `linkers/type_hierarchy.py:113` gates virtual dispatch on `child_kind == "interface"` alone, so Rust `trait` and Swift `protocol` fall outside a rule the neighbouring linker gets right. The suspicion was that `class` is apex/peer overloaded — naming both a concrete class and an abstract type — which would have meant folding values, an ADR-0027 amendment, and `stable_id` churn behind the v9/v10 scheme gate. **Measured across 11 languages, it is not:** every analyzer emits `class` for a class *declaration*, and abstract-ness is carried on a different field — `Symbol.modifiers == ['abstract']` in java/csharp/php/scala/kotlin, derivable from `meta.base_classes` in python. All six values in scope are **CANONICAL**; no fold, no ADR change, no scheme gating.
  - **The defect is enumeration-completeness, which the four leakage tests are explicitly not designed to catch.** `Symbol.kind` carries 138 values on exactly one axis (`language_construct`), so `symbol_kinds_on_axis()` cannot express "the abstract types" — unlike `Edge.edge_type`, whose ADR tells consumers outright to call `edge_types_on_axis(...)` instead of maintaining their own list. With no expressible grouping, every consumer writes a literal. An AST walk over every `packages/*/src` set literal found **47 sites carrying ≥2 type-like kinds, 26 under a strict all-members-registered filter, spanning 24 distinct vocabularies** — against a manual sweep that had found 7.
  - **`protocol` is missing from five language-agnostic sites** (`type_hierarchy.py:493`, `inheritance.py:426`, `_transitive_bases.py:51`, `di_resolution.py:669`, `cli.py:6629`) while three others are complete — so `inheritance.py` holds a correct set at line 91 and an incorrect one at line 426. Per-language analyzer sets are legitimately incomplete (Java has no traits) and are excluded. **Proven consequence, same construct one word apart:** a Java `interface` yields both `implements` and `dispatches_to: Shape.area -> Square.area`; a Swift `protocol` yields `implements` and **no dispatch at all**. The propagation mechanism is recorded in a comment — `type_hierarchy.py:493` says the set was copied "to match the inheritance linker's broader definition," and the copy inherited the omission. WI-duguk added `protocol` to two of the seven sites the day before and swept none of the rest.
  - **A second, orthogonal gap:** `modifiers` is the right home for abstract-ness but is under-populated — typescript emits `abstract class` with `modifiers=[]`, and cpp emits a pure-virtual class with `modifiers=[]` while dropping abstract method declarations entirely. Remedy is a registry-backed type-family predicate (the `Symbol.kind` analogue of `edge_types_on_axis()`), a sweep of the five sites, and a linter so copying a sibling's literal cannot recur — **not** a new axis under ADR-0024, since no field and no value classification changes.

### Fixed
- **The G1 validator ratchet was RED on `dev` and no per-PR run reported it (INV-fimon).** `test_validation_report_empty.py::test_substrate_within_ratchet_baseline` failed on all four substrates, carrying **2** un-allow-listed ADR-0023 §3 `runtime_coherence` offenders against a shrink-only baseline of **1**, alongside a stale Go route-extraction assertion. Neither is a new regression and both were invisible for the same reason: smart-test selects `test_validation_report_empty.py` only when a file it *imports* changes, and the manifest committed on `dev` did not contain it, so every merged PR passed CI over a red gate until an unrelated annotation batch touched `cli.py` and pulled it into scope. This is INV-kinin's first measured consequence with a blocked PR attached — a gate whose selection is content-dependent reports green for the wrong reason.
  - **Provenance measured, not inferred.** The second offender, `(trait, rust) → (method, rust)` carrying both `contains` and `dispatches_to`, was introduced by **WI-duguk (`9baaa9afbd`, "emit enum variants and trait members")**: checking out that commit's parent and re-running the corpus yields exactly **1** offender, the declared `(file, rust) → (external_symbol, rust)` one. The item's own framing ("not clear what was producing it"; "pre-existing") was right about the counts and wrong about the attribution window — the edge predates the *session* that found it, not the *change* that caused it.
  - **Ruled legitimate and allow-listed, deliberately narrowly (ADR-0023 §3 amendment).** The two edge types do not even share a destination — `contains` names the trait's own requirement (`MyTrait::method`), `dispatches_to` names the implementation (`MyStruct::method`) — so this is two distinct relationships over one endpoint shape, the same reasoning that admitted the file-anchor entries, not an endpoint property leaking into `edge_type`. The entry rules on the **partition only** and explicitly does not ratify the anchoring that made it necessary; the follow-up fix would *shrink* the allow-list rather than contradict it. The baseline stays at 1 and was **not** raised — a shrink-only baseline lifted to accept a breach ratifies the drift.
  - **Root cause is one level deeper, and is the familiar shape: one fact, two homes (INV-tihim).** `linkers/type_hierarchy` is the framework-agnostic linker that emits interface-method → impl-method `dispatches_to` for every language, and it **cannot fire for Rust at all**: its `_get_method_short_name` / `_get_class_name_from_method` split on `#` and `.` but not `::`, so a `MyTrait::method` returns `owner=None`, while `linkers/containment`'s `_SEPARATORS` knows all three. `linkers/rust_trait_dispatch` exists to cover that gap, and because trait members did not exist when it was written it anchors dispatch at the **trait** rather than at the trait's requirement. Its docstring said so — *"hypergumbo's Rust analyzer does not emit the trait's required-method list, so distinguishing trait-required methods from inherent methods would require SCIP-level information"* — and WI-duguk made that false. The docstring now records the supersession instead of the stale rationale.
  - **A second stale rationale removed.** The in-test note explaining why the surviving offender was pinned in the baseline rather than allow-listed said doing so would be a "governance-touching ADR-0023 allow-list amendment." It is not: neither `docs/adr/**` nor `docs/edge-type-runtime-allowlist.yaml` is on the AGENTS.md governance list. That sentence is what made the breach look unfixable in-band.
  - **The Go assertion tests a convention that no longer exists.** `BRANCHES_test_go.py::TestGoRouteExtraction::test_route_handler_as_selector` read `"GetUser" in n["name"]`; WI-zugob moved route-marker names to `"{METHOD} {path}"` (so multi-method registrations at one span keep distinct ids) and handler identity to `meta`. It now asserts on `meta["handler_name"]` — measured, not assumed: the item proposed `meta["handler_ref"]`, which is the key *other* producers use and does not exist on the Go path. Every other route-name assertion in the tree already reads the post-WI-zugob convention.
- **Scala, C++ and Kotlin emit enum members — the three languages the G2 parity matrix structurally cannot see (WI-dorop).** Each emitted the enum container and none of its members, so the containment linker had nothing to root and a reverse slice from the enum returned the container alone: the WI-duguk defect, in the analyzers that sit outside the gated set. Measured end-to-end, all three go **`contains_out` 0 → 3** and **`slice --entry Color --reverse` 1 node → 4**. Member naming follows each analyzer's own convention, because the containment linker splits on the first separator it finds: scala `Color.Red`, cpp `Color::Red`, kotlin `Color.RED`.
  - **Kotlin's container is a `class`, not an `enum`** — `enum class` is emitted as `kind="class"` with an `enum` modifier, and there is no `kind="enum"` anywhere in that analyzer. The member loop therefore gates **structurally on the presence of an `enum_class_body` node**, not on the kind or the modifier string. `class` is in both `CONTAINER_KINDS` and the slicer's set so the members root correctly without any kind change — but anything keyed on the enum kind (including the parity matrix's own `_ENUM_CONTAINER_KINDS`) will never see a Kotlin enum, which is why this was verified by containment count rather than an enum-kind query.
  - **Two per-language traps, each of which would have shipped a partial fix.** Scala has *two* member node types (`simple_enum_case` and `full_enum_case`), and `case Green, Blue` is a single `enum_case_definitions` holding two siblings — so the walk iterates cases, not groups; a per-group loop silently drops every case after a comma. C++'s emission sits deliberately *inside* the `if name_node:` guard: an anonymous `typedef enum { P, Q } Anon;` emits no container, so emitting its members would produce symbols whose owner does not exist — orphans by construction, worse than the recall miss.
  - One existing test changed: `test_enum_body_val_emits_field` asserted `_fields(syms) == {"Color.rgb"}` over a fixture that always contained `case Red`. The case now emits, so the set gains `Color.Red` — the assertion was pinning the defect.
- **The G2 parity matrix's scope is now declared and machine-enforced, and the false claim that gated it is corrected (WI-duguk follow-up).** The matrix carried the comment *"All eight are availability='core' (no grammar wheel needed)"*, and it was read as the reason the gate stops where it does — extending it looked like it would drag in optional dependencies. Measured against the live catalog, **only `python` is `availability="core"`**; java, javascript, go, rust, csharp and swift are all `"extra"`, the *same tier* as the scala/kotlin/cpp analyzers the matrix does **not** gate, whose grammars are hard dependencies of `hypergumbo-lang-mainstream` and install wherever it does. Two unrelated senses of "core" had collided — the catalog's "needs no extra dependency" versus the file's prose "the important ones" — and the collision manufactured a dependency barrier that does not exist. The real boundary is **7 of 38 mainstream analyzers, chosen historically and never argued for in an ADR**.
  - **Scope is now three declared maps plus a test that they exhaust the tier.** `UNGATED_CODE_LANGUAGES` names the 14 mainstream code languages this gate says nothing about (php, ruby, scala, kotlin, cpp, groovy, objc, c, bash, lua, perl, powershell, sql, jupyter); `NON_CONSTRUCT_FORMATS` names the 17 config/data/markup/build formats with no class-or-callable surface for the uniform construct set to be uniform *about* — because a row made almost entirely of `COLUMN_APPLICABILITY` exemptions is where a real gap hides behind a plausible excuse. `test_matrix_scope_is_declared_and_exhaustive` fails when a mainstream analyzer appears in none of the three, so analyzer #39 forces a one-line classification at the cheapest possible moment, and a stale entry naming a deleted analyzer fails too.
